1	# Cycle protocol
2	
3	This protocol uses [Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form). It
4	contains instructions on how a commit cycle is accomplished.
5	
6	The artifact a cycle produces is whatever the bot generates from
7	the conversation — code, prose, an image, a song, a screenplay.
8	The steps below use a Rust crate as the running example (the
9	cargo cycle, `Cargo.toml` versioning); substitute your medium's
10	equivalents — this project's manifest is recorded in
11	[versioning.md](versioning.md).
12	
13	## Cycles
14	
15	A cycle has three phases:
16	
17	- **[Preparation](#preparation)** (`X.Y.Z-0`) — the cycle's
18	  first commit, when it needs setup (a lightweight cycle omits
19	  it and starts at `-1` — see
20	  [versioning.md](versioning.md#step-numbering)). Sets up the
21	  cycle:
22	  - Bump the version-of-record to `X.Y.Z-0` (where it lives
23	    and the suffix scheme are project-specific — see
24	    [versioning.md](versioning.md)).
25	  - Pick up a `## Todo` item (typically the top-ranked,
26	    #1) into `## In Progress` (bold title + succinct problem
27	    statement + ladder).
28	  - Open the [chores section](#chores-sections).
29	- **[Work-N](#work-n)** (`X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, …) — the
30	  commits that implement the change. As many as the change
31	  needs; each runs through the
32	  [per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow).
33	- **[Close-out](#close-out)** (bare `X.Y.Z`) — the cycle's
34	  last commit. Bookkeeping only:
35	  - Move the picked-up item to a one-line entry in
36	    `## Done`.
37	  - Move the `## In Progress` block into the
38	    [chores section](#chores-sections).
39	  - Optionally update `notes/README.md` if functionality
40	    changed.
41	
42	A cycle's commits are published to the project remote
43	either incrementally or as one batch at close-out; the
44	result must always be published at close-out. See
45	[Pushing](#pushing).
46	
47	**Sub-cycles.** When a Work commit's scope grows enough to
48	warrant its own ladder, it subdivides — `X.Y.Z-3.0`
49	Preparation, `X.Y.Z-3.1` / `X.Y.Z-3.2` Work, `X.Y.Z-3`
50	Close-out. The same three-phase shape applies recursively
51	at every depth. See [Numbering](#numbering) for the
52	suffix rule and [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders)
53	for the local-ladder mechanics.
54	
55	## Chores sections
56	
57	A **chores section** is a `##` section in
58	`notes/chores/chores-NN.md` recording landed work. In
59	general, every commit that lands on the permanent branch
60	should have a reference to it on a `Commits:` list in a
61	chores file.
62	
63	The phrase **"Open" the chores section** means append a
64	`##` header to the current `notes/chores/chores-NN.md`
65	with the title it records (e.g. `## refactor: foo bar`),
66	followed by an **empty `Commits:`** line. The narrative
67	body stays empty until close-out (see [Close-out](#close-out));
68	the `Commits:` line is backfilled later, once the commit is
69	permanent (see [Commits backfill](#commits-backfill) below).
70	
71	Fuller chores conventions (content rules, header sync,
72	design subsection pattern, `Commits:` formatting) live in
73	AGENTS.md [Chores conventions](../AGENTS.md#chores-conventions).
74	
75	### Commits backfill
76	
77	A chores section's `Commits:` line cites the commit(s) it
78	records, by SHA — but a SHA isn't stable until the commit
79	lands on a **permanent branch** (`main`, or a long-lived
80	release/patch branch that won't be rewritten); a rebase or
81	squash rewrites it on the way. So:
82	
83	- A chores section is **opened with an empty `Commits:`** line.
84	- **Backfill once the commit is on a permanent branch**,
85	  where its SHA is final. A commit can't record its own SHA
86	  (that would change the hash), so the fill always lands one
87	  push later: **each push backfills the `Commits:` of the
88	  commits the previous push made permanent.** On a topic
89	  branch the sections instead wait until the branch lands —
90	  so no SHA is ever written that a later rebase could
91	  invalidate.
92	
93	Use `[[N]]` refs — several as `[[N]],[[M]]` only when one
94	section records multiple commits (a merge non-ff close-out) —
95	with the commit URL + 40-hex SHA in the file's `# References`
96	(format in AGENTS.md
97	[Chores commit references](../AGENTS.md#chores-commit-references)).
98	A section's `##` title matches its commit title, so a rare
99	deliberate rewrite of a permanent-branch commit re-syncs via
100	`git log --grep "<title>"`.
101	
102	The per-push cadence is a project choice, not dogma — a
103	**per-close-out** model (recording a cycle's SHAs at its
104	close-out) is equally valid. The one invariant: a recorded
105	SHA must be permanent.
106	
107	## Preparation
108	
109	The cycle's first commit (`X.Y.Z-0`), when the cycle needs
110	setup (a lightweight cycle omits it — see
111	[versioning.md](versioning.md#step-numbering)):
112	
113	- **Bump the version-of-record** to `X.Y.Z-0`. Where it
114	  lives, the suffix scheme, and any derived files (a
115	  lockfile, a sourced manifest version) are
116	  project-specific — see [versioning.md](versioning.md).
117	- **Move a `## Todo` item** (if the cycle has one) into
118	  `## In Progress` and the todo item should have:
119	  - A **bold title line** — that will be the chores
120	    section header, minus the `## ` prefix.
121	  - A **succinct problem statement**; add if one is needed
122	  - A **ladder**.
123	- **Open the [chores section](#chores-sections)** —
124	  append a `##` header with the title it records — the
125	  cycle's anticipated close-out title.
126	
127	## Work-N
128	
129	The cycle's work commits (`X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, …)
130	implement the change. As many as needed:
131	
132	- Each commit runs through the
133	  **[per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow)**.
134	- **Interim pushes** are optional (backup, progress
135	  visibility).
136	- Close-out is the only mandatory push (see
137	  [Pushing](#pushing)).
138	- **Subdivide into a sub-cycle** if a Work commit's
139	  scope grows enough (see
140	  [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders)).
141	
142	## Close-out
143	
144	The cycle's last commit (bare `X.Y.Z`) does bookkeeping
145	only — the commit body describes that bookkeeping, not
146	what happens post-squash:
147	
148	- **Move the picked-up item** from `## In Progress` to a
149	  one-line entry (with a chores `[N]` ref) in `## Done`.
150	- **Move the `## In Progress` block into the
151	  [chores section](#chores-sections)** (the title-only
152	  placeholder opened during Preparation):
153	  - Cut the problem statement + ladder out of
154	    `## In Progress` (drop the bold title line — the
155	    chores `##` header already carries that string).
156	  - Paste under the chores `##` header.
157	  - Sync the chores header to the **final** commit title
158	    if the cycle's scope shifted; update every anchor
159	    back-reference.
160	  - Add an `### As-built ladder` listing the cycle's
161	    commits; optional `### Outcome` notes.
162	  - Replace `## In Progress` with
163	    `_No cycle currently in progress._`.
164	- **Update `notes/README.md`** if functionality changed
165	  (new flags, new subcommands, changed behavior).
166	
167	Whether to **squash** the cycle into one commit before the
168	publishing push, or push as-is, is decided at push time —
169	see [Pushing](#pushing).
170	
171	## Numbering
172	
173	Each commit's phase is encoded in the version suffix — `-0`
174	Preparation, `-1`/`-2`/… Work, bare `X.Y.Z` Close-out,
175	recursively for sub-cycles. The full scheme — disambiguation,
176	nesting, optional Preparation, the project's version-of-record
177	format, and the per-phase bump — lives in
178	[versioning.md](versioning.md#step-numbering), which is the
179	single source of truth for this repo's versioning.
180	
181	## Per-commit flow
182	
183	Every commit (Preparation, each Work commit, Close-out) goes
184	through:
185	
186	1. **Mark this commit `(current)`** as the first edit in
187	   `notes/todo.md > ## In Progress`.
188	2. **Do the work** (see [Iterative work](#iterative-work)
189	   for the loop-and-squash technique).
190	3. **Flip this commit `(current)` → `(done)`** in `## In
191	   Progress`.
192	4. **Validate the artifact** — a medium-specific step, skip-able
193	   for notes-only commits, mandatory at close-out. For the Rust
194	   example the cargo cycle is:
195	   1. `cargo fmt`
196	   2. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`
197	   3. `cargo test`
198	   4. `cargo install --path . --locked`
199	   5. (re-test if anything substantive changed)
200	5. **Work review.** Stop *before* writing any description;
201	   tell the user "ready to commit." The user reviews the
202	   changes and we iterate until complete.
203	6. **Write the commit description** — see
204	   [Commit description](#commit-description).
205	7. **Commit Description review.** Show the title + body
206	   and stop. The user reviews the description. Iterate.
207	8. **Commit.** `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R .` for
208	   the app repo, `-R .claude` for the bot repo (`-R` last
209	   keeps the verb visible):
210	
211	   ```
212	   jj commit -m \
213	   "<type>: <short description>" \
214	   -m "<intro paragraph>
215	
216	   - file1: gist
217	   - file2: gist
218	
219	   ochid: /.claude/<chid>" \
220	   -R .
221	   ```
222	
223	**Two overrides apply:**
224	
225	- **Deviation or question** — any time the work deviates
226	  from the agreed plan, or a question arises, stop and
227	  surface it; don't push through.
228	- **ESC-ESC** — the user can interrupt at any point to pull
229	  a review or question forward.
230	
231	## Commit description
232	
233	[Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/):
234	
235	```
236	<type>: <short description>
237	```
238	
239	Titles carry **no trailing `(<version>)` suffix**. The
240	version-of-record — where it lives and its bump cadence, see
241	[versioning.md](versioning.md) — is useful for confirming
242	you're running the version you're testing, not a per-commit
243	title marker. We think per-commit version-in-title is
244	unstable on large projects: many changes are in flight
245	simultaneously, and a
246	version is only stable once merged into the main repo — so
247	titles don't carry one.
248	
249	### Title
250	
251	- ≤50 chars total.
252	- Common types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `test`,
253	  `docs`, `chore`.
254	- Favor terse phrasings.
255	
256	### Body
257	
258	[Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form) (intro + bullets),
259	wrap ≤72. Bullet content differs per repo:
260	
261	- **App-repo body**: file-by-file. One bullet per file
262	  changed (file plus a one-line gist). Sub-bullets for
263	  files with multiple distinct changes:
264	
265	  ```
266	  - path/to/file1
267	    - first distinct change
268	    - second distinct change, wrapping to next line
269	       with continuation indented 5
270	  ```
271	
272	  The file-by-file list is the source of truth for the
273	  cycle's mechanical change record. Chores carries the
274	  narrative + design, not a copy of it. Promote any
275	  "why" beyond one sentence to a chores `###` subsection.
276	
277	- **Session-repo (`.claude`) body**: bullets describe
278	  in-session activity rather than code changes.
279	
280	### Trailer
281	
282	`ochid:` as the last line of the body — see
283	[Cross-repo linking (ochid trailers)](../AGENTS.md#cross-repo-linking-ochid-trailers)
284	in AGENTS.md for the convention.
285	
286	For breaking changes, use the hyphenated `BREAKING-CHANGE:`
287	trailer key. `BREAKING CHANGE:` (with a space) is the only
288	space-separated key the Conventional Commits spec allows; the
289	hyphenated form is also valid and avoids the space ambiguity.
290	
291	## Reviewing changes
292	
293	Work review looks at the **uncommitted working-copy diff**,
294	on the way to commit. The user opens diffs in their
295	editor (Zed, VSCode); jj commands are for terminal:
296	
297	- `jj diff` — working-copy diff (uncommitted)
298	- `jj diff -r @-` — diff of the previous commit
299	- `jj diff --from <X> --to <Y>` — any two revisions
300	- `jj show -r <X>` — description + diff for one rev
301	
302	Don't `jj edit -r @-` to view a past commit — that marks
303	it mutable and shifts `@`; use `jj diff -r @-` or
304	`jj show -r @-`.
305	
306	See [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders) for the
307	close-out squash recipe and recovery; revset primitives
308	are in [`jj-tips.md`](jj-tips.md#revsets).
309	
310	## Pushing
311	
312	### Policy
313	
314	Push is **discretionary** during the cycle (backup,
315	progress visibility) and **mandatory at close-out** —
316	the cycle's result must be published.
317	
318	**Approval is per-push.** Every push — any repo, any kind:
319	cycle push, interim backup, recovery/surgery force-push —
320	happens only after the user has reviewed the changes to be
321	published and explicitly approved that specific push.
322	Approval of a plan that *includes* a push does not authorize
323	the push itself; stop and ask again at the moment of pushing.
324	
325	**Default is interactive; an explicit scoped delegation waives
326	the gates.** The gates above — per-push approval, the
327	commit-description review (show title+body and stop), and the
328	hard stop after push/finalize — are the *interactive default*.
329	They yield when the user **explicitly** delegates a complete,
330	bounded task and authorizes carrying it through ("do all of X
331	and push each step, don't check in"). The bot then proceeds
332	through that task's commits and pushes without stopping, and
333	continues past each push to the next step. Conditions:
334	
335	- **Explicit grant** — never inferred from a task merely being
336	  well-scoped; the user's words must authorize unattended
337	  completion.
338	- **Bounded goal** — covers the named task only; does not carry
339	  to the next task or a vaguer follow-on.
340	- **Destructive ops still pause** — delegation covers the task's
341	  ordinary commits and pushes; it does *not* pre-authorize a
342	  genuinely irreversible action (force-push over published
343	  history, history rewrite, deleting a remote branch). Those can
344	  permanently destroy work and aren't a normal cycle step, so the
345	  bot flags one before acting. An ordinary delegated cycle never
346	  reaches this.
347	- **Still transparent** — report each commit/push as it lands
348	  (title + outcome) so the user can catch up.
349	- **When in doubt, ask** — ambiguous authorization falls back to
350	  per-push approval.
351	
352	### Shape at close-out push
353	
354	At close-out the cycle's *work* is done; its *published
355	shape* is the remaining choice, made at push time. Surface
356	the options and get user approval before pushing — once on
357	the target, changing shape is a remote rewrite (force-push,
358	needs approval), so choose deliberately.
359	
360	- **Squash to one commit** — single entry on the target.
361	  Right for straightforward changes where the Work-N is
362	  focused on one or two files.
363	- **Merge non-ff** *(current default)* — `main` gains a
364	  merge commit (`X.Y.Z`); cycle commits stay reachable via
365	  two parents. `jj log -r ..@ -n <N>` shows the trapezoidal
366	  shape. See [Merge non-ff recipe](#merge-non-ff-recipe)
367	  for the full setup sequence.
368	- **Keep separate** — one commit per cycle entry on
369	  `main`. Use when the decomposition itself is
370	  informative. Each chores section keeps its own header /
371	  `Commits:` ref; no consolidation churn.
372	
373	Set up squash/merge before invoking `vc-x1 push`; use
374	`jj git push` directly for non-standard shapes.
375	
376	### Merge non-ff recipe
377	
378	Setting up a [Merge non-ff](#shape-at-close-out-push)
379	close-out is a fixed sequence. `<closeout>` is the cycle's
380	close-out commit, `<prev>` the previous cycle's close-out
381	(the current `main` tip), `<work-tip>` the cycle's last
382	Work commit:
383	
384	1. **Rebase the close-out into a merge** — `jj rebase -r
385	   <closeout> --onto <prev> --onto <work-tip>`.
386	   - `-r <closeout>` keeps the `<closeout>` commit in place.
387	   - `--onto <prev>` becomes its first parent (trunk).
388	   - `--onto <work-tip>` becomes the second parent.
389	
390	   Together these make `<closeout>` a merge of
391	   `<prev>` + `<work-tip>`, forming a trapezoidal commit.
392	2. **Use `jj new <merge>`** to add an empty `@` above the
393	   merge. The rebase left `@` *on* the now-content-bearing
394	   merge, which git/IDE diff views show as uncommitted;
395	   `jj new` restores the clean empty `@` on top.
396	3. **Push** — `jj git push --bookmark main -R .`.
397	
398	**Post-hoc caveat.** If the cycle was already pushed
399	[Keep separate](#shape-at-close-out-push) its commits are
400	immutable: the rebase needs `--ignore-immutable` and the
401	push force-updates `main`.
402	The standard sequence assumes the merge is set up *before*
403	the close-out push.
404	
405	### vc-x1 push wrapper
406	
407	`vc-x1 push <bookmark>` wraps per-push mechanics. See
408	`vc-x1 push --help` for current flags.
409	
410	**Current limitation**: only fully supports the
411	[Keep separate](#shape-at-close-out-push) shape; other
412	shapes need manual jj steps. Improvements tracked /
413	planned:
414	
415	- N:1 code↔bot for Merge non-ff (`## Todo` entry P1).
416	- Symmetric squash (planned, to be captured in `-2`).
417	- Per-repo bookmark names (planned).
418	
419	### .claude cadence
420	
421	**Cadence**: one push = one `.claude` commit, paired
422	with every code commit in that push.
423	
424	The `.claude` working copy accumulates session data
425	across the cycle; its change ID stays stable across
426	snapshots, `jj describe`, and the finalize commit, so
427	code-side `ochid:` trailers resolve.
428	
429	`.claude` is a linear journal — all session work lives
430	on `main`, regardless of the app-side bookmark. **Do not
431	create or maintain `.claude` bookmarks that mirror
432	app-side branches** — risks the bot steering session
433	pushes to the wrong remote ref.
434	
435	Ending a session: if the user runs `/exit` there will be
436	session information created, which we don't worry about.
437	The user can close the terminal instead and `@` will
438	remain empty.
439	
440	### Bot communication at the reviews
441	
442	Use plain prose — no insider jargon ("Gate N signal",
443	"Checkpoint N", etc.):
444	
445	- **At Work review** — summarize what changed and stop.
446	  "Work complete. Please review."
447	- **At Commit Description review** — present `$TITLE`
448	  and `$BODY` explicitly; ask permission to commit/push.
449	  Don't spell out the full `vc-x1 push ... --title ...
450	  --body ...` invocation by default.
451	- **At Post close-out review** — surface the shape
452	  options (squash / merge / keep) and the push target;
453	  wait for the user's choice before any `jj squash` /
454	  `jj rebase` / `jj git push` invocation.
455	
456	### After push or finalize: stop and wait
457	
458	After you **push** (cross the remote boundary) or launch
459	**finalize** on the `.claude` repo (`vc-x1 finalize --repo
460	.claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach`) — by
461	hand or via the `vc-x1 push` wrapper — you **MUST NEVER**
462	proceed (next step, edit, tool call, text output) until the
463	user explicitly directs you to continue. **Even when the next
464	step seems obvious — wait.** Treat push-or-finalize as a hard
465	stop for the whole turn.
466	
467	`vc-x1 push` performs both as its tail stages — `push-app`
468	then the detached `vc-x1 finalize` on `.claude` — so there is
469	no gap left to speak in once the push is invoked. Put **all**
470	closing words *before* invoking it. The harness rejects an empty
471	turn, so it may force a visible token after the tool returns; if
472	so, emit a bare acknowledgment only (e.g. "landed") — never a
473	summary, verification, or next-step offer.
474	
475	Why (`.claude` is the live journal finalize snapshots →
476	squashes → pushes):
477	
478	- **`--detach`**: lets the tool return immediately so the
479	  harness can keep flushing the *trailing* session-data — the
480	  tool call's own transcript and any words emitted *before*
481	  the invoke — into the snapshot.
482	- **`--delay 10`**: gives the harness time to complete that
483	  flush before the snapshot is taken. It is **not** time for
484	  the bot to do more work.
485	- **silence**: the bot must not summarize or perform any work
486	  after the tool returns. The harness may force a visible token
487	  (it rejects an empty turn) — keep it to a bare acknowledgment,
488	  never a summary or a next step.
489	
490	**Known slip**: the bot has emitted a summary after launching
491	finalize. There is no "harmless" closing line — if it's worth
492	saying, say it *before* the invoke.
493	
494	### Recovery
495	
496	- **If push exits before `finalize-claude`** (e.g.
497	  failure between `push-app` and `finalize-claude`), run
498	  finalize by hand:
499	
500	  ```
501	  vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
502	  ```
503	
504	- **Run finalize again if `@` is non-empty** after the
505	  finalize's squash-and-commit (also desirable after
506	  extra activity by the bot's agents).
507	  - Why: finalize snapshots `.claude` at `--delay`, but the
508	    bot keeps writing session data while it runs — the tool's
509	    own invocation plus the bot's closing response land
510	    *after* the snapshot.
511	  - Safe to repeat: bot session data is append-only, so a
512	    re-run never conflicts or overwrites. (This could
513	    change; it is not under the user's control.)
514	  - Fix: run `vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push
515	    <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach` another time to capture
516	    that tail; one extra pass is generally enough.
517	  - No guarantees: events outside the bot's control can leave
518	    `@` non-empty — e.g. the bot's back end may decide to
519	    squash/consolidate session data, which can take minutes
520	    and land after the snapshot. The remedy is the same: just
521	    run finalize again. This is why a single pass is never
522	    guaranteed to leave `@` empty.
523	- **Clear push's saved state** after any out-of-band
524	  recovery — `rm .vc-x1/push-state.toml` or `vc-x1 push
525	  <bookmark> --restart` — otherwise push resumes from a
526	  stale stage.
527	- **Late code-repo tweak after `push-app` succeeded**
528	  (e.g. updating AGENTS.md or memory) requires `jj
529	  squash --ignore-immutable` and a re-push; that is a
530	  remote rewrite and needs explicit approval like any
531	  push.
532	
533	## Iterative work
534	
535	When work for a single commit (the **target**) benefits
536	from incremental review, loop:
537	
538	1. `jj new -R .` — fresh empty `@` on top of the target.
539	2. Make the next round of changes.
540	3. User reviews the round (see
541	   [Reviewing changes](#reviewing-changes)).
542	4. `jj squash -R .` folds into the target and creates a
543	   new empty `@`.
544	5. If not done, go to step 2.
545	
546	Same jj mechanics as a
547	[sub-cycle ladder](#sub-cycle-ladders), but at
548	single-commit scope — the version
549	doesn't change.
550	
551	## Sub-cycle ladders
552	
553	A **ladder** is a series of steps — a cycle's Work
554	commits, built as a local jj `@` chain. The structure is
555	recursive:
556	
557	- A **step** is one Work commit.
558	- Any step can subdivide into its **own ladder** of steps
559	  (a sub-cycle). The version suffix nests to match —
560	  `…-3`, then `…-3.1`, `…-3.2`; see [Numbering](#numbering).
561	- You **return** from a subdivision by **closing out**
562	  that ladder: its steps are collected back under one
563	  Close-out, then the enclosing ladder continues.
564	
565	While a ladder is being built its steps are provisional —
566	working titles, rewritten in place — there to sub-divide
567	the work into pieces you can review in isolation. At each
568	close-out you decide whether to squash the steps into one
569	commit or push them as-is (see [Pushing](#pushing)).
570	
571	### Per-step contract
572	
573	For each step in the ladder:
574	
575	1. `jj new -R .` — create a fresh empty `@`.
576	2. Do the step's work.
577	3. Run the fast validation (Rust example: `cargo test
578	   --bins`). **Non-negotiable** — for code, build and clippy
579	   alone miss regressions until a later step runs the full
580	   suite, by which point it's harder to tell which step
581	   introduced them.
582	4. `jj describe -m "..." -m "..." -R .` — a provisional
583	   title: the final description supersedes it if the
584	   ladder is squashed at Close-out, but it stands if the
585	   ladder is pushed unsquashed — so keep it presentable.
586	
587	### Navigating the ladder
588	
589	Common moves:
590	
591	- `jj log -r '<base>::' -R .` — see the whole ladder
592	  from its base.
593	- `jj edit -r <prefix> -R .` — jump `@` to any ladder
594	  step by chid prefix; handy for tracking down which
595	  step caused a problem.
596	- `jj edit @-- -R .` — quick-jump back two steps.
597	- `jj diff -r <chid> -R .` — review one step in
598	  isolation.
599	
600	Modifications to any ladder step rewrite it in place;
601	descendants auto-rebase.
602	
603	### Close-out: squash or push as-is
604	
605	When all ladder steps are done and tests pass,
606	decide whether to squash (per [Pushing](#pushing)).
607	
608	To **squash** into one commit:
609	
610	```
611	jj squash --from "<base>..@-" --into @ -u -R .
612	```
613	
614	`<base>` is the parent of the first ladder step; `-u`
615	keeps `@`'s description and discards the sources'.
616	After squash, history is linear: `<base> → @`;
617	intermediate steps are auto-abandoned.
618	
619	For N = 1 the squash is a no-op (`<base>..@-` is empty
620	when `@-` is `<base>`); push the single step directly.
621	
622	To **push as-is**, skip the squash and leave the chain
623	intact — each ladder step ships as its own commit, so
624	give every one a presentable description first.
625	
626	Either way, finish with `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` as for
627	any other commit.
628	
629	### Recovery
630	
631	If a ladder step goes wrong, back out without losing
632	prior steps:
633	
634	- **Discard the current step.** `jj abandon @ -R .`
635	  drops it; you get a fresh empty `@` on the same
636	  parent.
637	- **Edit an earlier step.** `jj edit -r <chid> -R .`,
638	  make corrections, then `jj edit -r <last-ladder-chid>`
639	  to return. Descendants auto-rebase.
640	- **Discard the entire ladder.** `jj op log -R .` shows
641	  the op history; `jj op restore <op-id> -R .` reverts
642	  to that point. Full undo — removes *all* ladder work
643	  after the chosen op. Use only to start over.
644	
645	# References
646	
647	- [`jj-tips.md`](jj-tips.md#revsets) — revset primitives
648	  (chid/cid, `@`/`@-`/`@+`, `..`/`::` ranges, prefix matching).
649	- [`substep-test.sh`](substep-test.sh) — script that
650	  scaffolds a 4-revision ladder under `/tmp/substep-test`
651	  for squash-recipe experiments.
652	- The per-commit testing gate exists because a
653	  regression introduced in an early ladder commit can go
654	  uncaught until a later commit runs the full suite, raising
655	  bisection cost.
656	
```

1	# bdimager
2	
3	When complete this Python tool will allow you to take a working
4	image from a block device, typically an sd-card, and
5	- Capture the contents to a file *mother* image
6	- Build this into a distributable(?) image
7	- Write the distributable image to a target block device
8	  and this target device can be a different size than
9	  the *mother* image.
10	
11	Eventually I'd like to convert this to Rust.
12	
13	## Repo
14	
15	This is the main repo of a dual-repo convention for using
16	a bot to help develop a project — code, but equally prose, an
17	image, a song, a screenplay, anything the bot generates from a
18	conversation. The goal is that this main repo contains the
19	"what" (the artifact), while the partner bot repo contains the
20	"why" and "how" (the conversation). The key to the convention is
21	each change is cross-referenced to the other. Thus there is a
22	coherent story of the development of the project across time.
23	
24	The beginnings of that tool is [vc-x1](https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1)
25	which currently does achieve this goal, but is being used as a
26	first test bed.
27	
28	### Assumptions
29	
30	- **The [`vc-x1`](https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1) companion tool**
31	  will be used to help manage the repo the underlying jj steps are
32	  documented so the flow also works without it.
33	
34	### Cloning
35	
36	`vc-x1 clone ..` can be used to clone
37	
38	## Releasing
39	
40	TBD
41	
42	## jj Tips for Git Users
43	
44	This project uses [Jujutsu (jj)](https://docs.jj-vcs.dev/latest/)
45	alongside git. New to jj? See
46	[Steve Klabnik](https://github.com/steveklabnik)'s
47	[Jujutsu tutorial](https://steveklabnik.github.io/jujutsu-tutorial).
48	
49	Repo-specific how-tos — initial commit, pushing, modifying and
50	force-pushing a commit, revsets, and a useful-commands reference —
51	live in [notes/jj-tips.md](notes/jj-tips.md).
52	
53	## Cross-repo Linking with Git Trailers
54	
55	Commits in each repo use [git trailers](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-interpret-trailers)
56	to cross-reference their counterpart in the other repo via an
57	`ochid` (Other Change ID) trailer — the defining mechanism of the
58	dual-repo convention. For the full definition — trailer syntax,
59	the example shape, per-commit mechanics, and `.vc-config.toml` —
60	see
61	[Cross-repo linking (ochid trailers)](AGENTS.md#cross-repo-linking-ochid-trailers).
62	
63	## Contributing
64	
65	Bot-following workflow, commit conventions, and code style are
66	canonical in [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md) (which `CLAUDE.md` imports)
67	and [notes/cycle-protocol.md](notes/cycle-protocol.md)
68	
69	- [Cycle numbering](notes/cycle-protocol.md#numbering) — the
70	  `X.Y.Z-N` phase-suffix convention (Preparation / Work /
71	  Close-out).
72	- [Commit description](notes/cycle-protocol.md#commit-description)
73	  — Conventional Commits + `(version)` suffix and per-repo body
74	  shape.
75	
76	Task tracking and release details live under [notes/](notes/):
77	near-term tasks in [notes/todo.md](notes/todo.md), per-release
78	details in `notes/chores/chores-*.md`, and notes-specific
79	formatting rules in [notes/README.md](notes/README.md).
80	
81	## License

86	- MIT license ([LICENSE-MIT](LICENSE-MIT) or http://opensource.org/licenses/MIT)

1	# Versioning
2	
3	How this project versions its commits and the running
4	artifact. The scheme — the cycle step-numbering and the
5	unique-per-commit aim — is generic and shared across projects:
6	this file is copied **verbatim**, with
7	[Recording the version-of-record](#recording-the-version-of-record)
8	covering each medium by conditional rather than per-project
9	edits.
10	
11	## Terms
12	
13	Three names, used as defined here across
14	[AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md),
15	[cycle-protocol.md](cycle-protocol.md), and the notes files:
16	
17	- **version** — the per-commit version (e.g. `0.3.0-5.3.0`),
18	  written with `-` in prose, ladders, chores, todo, and commit
19	  talk; its suffix encodes the cycle phase (see
20	  [Step numbering](#step-numbering)).
21	- **version-of-record** — the authoritative stored copy of the
22	  version, in the project's manifest (see
23	  [Recording the version-of-record](#recording-the-version-of-record));
24	  a built or running artifact derives from it.
25	- **versioning** — the topic: this scheme as a whole.
26	
27	## Recording the version-of-record
28	
29	Where the version-of-record lives, how it's stored and
30	surfaced, and how often it changes — pick the case that fits
31	your medium:
32	
33	- **Manifest** — where the version-of-record is stored:
34	  - if Rust, `Cargo.toml` `[package].version`
35	  - if Python, `pyproject.toml` `[project].version` (or the
36	    committed config it's sourced from)
37	  - otherwise wherever the medium records it (a generic
38	    `version.toml`, a book's frontmatter, …) — add the case as
39	    needed
40	- **Notation** — how the `-` form is stored:
41	  - if the format allows `-` (TOML `version.toml`, `Cargo.toml`),
42	    store it verbatim
43	  - if it bars `-` (PEP 440's local segment, e.g. a Python
44	    project), remap to `+` — `0.3.0-5.3.0` → `0.3.0+5.3.0`: same
45	    version, just the stored spelling
46	- **Reporter** — how a built artifact surfaces the
47	  version-of-record:
48	  - if a CLI app, `<cli-app> -V`
49	  - if a TUI/GUI, add to Help/About or display on the title
50	- **Cadence** — how often to bump: see
51	  [Unique per commit](#unique-per-commit-preference-not-requirement);
52	  this project follows the per-commit preference.
53	
54	## Unique per commit (preference, not requirement)
55	
56	Our general notion is that the version-of-record should change
57	on **every commit**, so a built or running artifact identifies
58	the exact commit it came from.
59	
60	- This is a preference, **not** a hard requirement. A project
61	  following the cycle protocol may bump less often — once per
62	  cycle, only at release, and so on. Record the choice in
63	  [Recording the version-of-record](#recording-the-version-of-record)
64	  if it differs.
65	- It is achievable because the cycle's versions (below) are
66	  **pre-assignable**, unlike the git SHA, which a commit cannot
67	  contain (see the cycle protocol's
68	  [Commits backfill](cycle-protocol.md#commits-backfill)).
69	
70	## Step numbering
71	
72	The cycle (Preparation -> Work -> Close-out; see
73	[cycle-protocol.md](cycle-protocol.md)) encodes each commit's
74	phase in the version suffix — the **final identifier `0` marks
75	a Preparation**:
76	
77	- `X.Y.Z-0` — Preparation
78	- `X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, … — Work commits
79	- `X.Y.Z` — Close-out (bare version, no suffix)
80	
81	**Preparation is optional.** A lightweight cycle — no ladder,
82	no setup commit — skips `-0` and starts at `-1` (its first
83	Work commit). The same holds at every level: a sub-cycle
84	needing no Preparation omits its `.0` (see Nesting). One that
85	grows a Preparation later adds the `0` step without
86	renumbering siblings.
87	
88	Disambiguation:
89	
90	- `-10` — Work commit #10 (final identifier `10`), not a
91	  Preparation.
92	- `-1.0` — Preparation of the `-1` sub-cycle (final
93	  identifier `0`).
94	
95	**Nesting.** Sub-cycles append another level, recursively:
96	
97	- `X.Y.Z-3.0` — Preparation of the `-3` sub-cycle
98	- `X.Y.Z-3.1`, `X.Y.Z-3.2` — its Work
99	- `X.Y.Z-3` — its Close-out
100	- `X.Y.Z-3.1.0` — Preparation of the `-3.1` sub-sub-cycle
101	
102	Bump the version-of-record at the start of each phase, so the
103	active phase is recorded — and, per the preference above,
104	every commit carries a distinct version.
105	
```

1	"""Shared config plumbing for the SD-card image-builder apps.
2	
3	The builder is split into standalone apps — `build` (rootless),
4	`capture`, and `write` — that each own their CLI (argparse) but share how
5	settings are sourced. This module is that shared layer:
6	
7	- load the app's `fc-config.toml` tables (e.g. `[image]` + `[build]`)
8	  over a built-in default map, erroring on an unknown key,
9	- merge file < `FC_<KEY>` env < CLI flag into a `{key: (value, source)}`
10	  plan so `--dry-run` can show where each value won,
11	- generate the per-key CLI flags and print the resolved settings block.
12	
13	Settings map 1:1 to CLI flags — identical names (`scrub_logs` <->
14	`--scrub-logs`). Precedence, lowest to highest: the config tables, then
15	an `FC_<KEY>` env var, then the matching CLI flag. The config file
16	defaults to `fc-config.toml` in the project root (`..` from this
17	module's source); each app's `--config` overrides it.
18	"""
19	
20	import argparse
21	import os
22	import tomllib
23	from pathlib import Path
24	
25	# Default config: fc-config.toml in the project root, i.e. `..` from this
26	# module's source (scripts/), the same file src/fc.py reads.
27	DEFAULT_CONFIG = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / "fc-config.toml"
28	
29	# Canonical schema: every valid key in each fc-config.toml table. Used to
30	# reject typos (an unknown key in a table) while still letting an app read
31	# only the subset of a shared table it needs — e.g. capture reads [image]
32	# but not its `output`, yet `output` must not look like a typo there.
33	TABLE_KEYS = {
34	    "image": {"images_dir", "mother", "output"},
35	    "build": {
36	        "fc_path", "fc_config_target", "autologin_tty",
37	        "scrub_logs", "scrub_ssh_host_keys", "scrub_machine_id",
38	        "scrub_shell_history", "scrub_wifi", "shrink", "compress",
39	    },
40	    "capture": {"source"},
41	    "write": {"target", "target_byid", "expand", "allow_yes"},
42	}
43	
44	# FC_* env values that count as true / false (case-insensitive). Anything
45	# else is a hard error so a typo never silently flips a toggle.
46	_TRUE = {"1", "true", "yes", "on"}
47	_FALSE = {"0", "false", "no", "off"}
48	
49	
50	def parse_bool(raw: str, source: str) -> bool:
51	    """Coerce an FC_* env string to bool, erroring on anything unknown.
52	
53	    - `source` names the variable for the error message,
54	    - accepts the _TRUE / _FALSE token sets, case-insensitively.
55	    """
56	    token = raw.strip().lower()
57	    if token in _TRUE:
58	        return True
59	    if token in _FALSE:
60	        return False
```

I've done the pre-plan reading (AGENTS.md, cycle-protocol.md, versioning.md, the bdimager docs) and scoped the source at `../fc`. Here's where I've landed before I draft the ladder.

## What I found

The "image builder code" in `../fc` is a clean, self-contained subsystem of 7 files under `scripts/` (~1,970 lines, pure stdlib + intra-package imports — no third-party deps):

- `imgcfg.py` — shared config plumbing: reads `fc-config.toml` `[image]/[build]/[capture]/[write]` tables, merges *file < `FC_<KEY>` env < CLI flag*, supports `--dry-run` showing where each value won.
- `imglib.py` — image library (leaf).
- `devsafe.py` — device-safety helpers (depends on `imglib`).
- `capture.py` — source block device → **mother** image (needs sudo; has `--test-mode`).
- `build.py` — mother → **deployable** image (rootless; `--shrink`/`--compress`).
- `write.py` — deploy image → target device (destructive; needs sudo; `--test-mode`; `--expand` to fill a larger target).
- `mk_test_image.py` — synthetic images for rootless self-tests.

This maps 1:1 onto bdimager's stated purpose (capture mother → build distributable → write to a possibly-larger target). **"Test the components"** is already solved upstream: there are no pytest files, but the `justfile` has rootless, no-hardware end-to-end self-tests — `image-test`, `capture-test`, `write-test` — driven by `mk_test_image.py` + `--test-mode`. That's the natural validation gate for this medium.

bdimager itself is currently bare — no `pyproject.toml`, no `src/`, no version-of-record yet. So Preparation has real setup to do.

## The tension I want you to resolve

A literal "copy" carries two fc-isms that may not belong in a tool you've described as a *generic* block-device imager:

1. **`build.py` bakes the fc app into the image** — `fc_path`, `fc_config_target`, `autologin_tty`, and a suite of `scrub_*` toggles (logs, ssh host keys, machine-id, wifi…). That's flight-campaign-specific provisioning, not generic imaging.
2. **fc naming everywhere** — `fc-config.toml`, the `FC_*` env prefix, `fc`-named tables.

My assumption on naming regardless of scope: rename to `bd-config.toml` + `BD_*` (keeping `FC_` in a repo called bdimager would just be confusing). I'll treat that as a default unless you object.

Two genuine forks remain, and they change the ladder enough that I'd rather ask than guess:

> AGENT

That reading makes sense, and I think I see the elegant bit: support **both** separators precisely so that when one collides, the other is your escape hatch. Let me lay out how I'd handle it and confirm I've got "collision" right.

## Explicit `src` → `dest` mapping

- Each `--add` entry (and each `@file` line) is a `source`→`destination` pair: host path on the left, path inside the image rootfs on the right.
- **Accept both separators, `:` and `->`.** Per-entry, you pick whichever doesn't appear in your paths — that's the collision escape hatch:
  - A `dest` like `/weird:name` → write it with `->`.
  - A path containing `->` (rare) → write it with `:`.
- **Ambiguity is a hard error, not a guess.** If a single entry is genuinely ambiguous — contains *both* separators, or the chosen separator appears more than once with no clear split — build refuses and tells you to disambiguate (e.g. use the other form). Fail loud beats silently mis-placing a file into a system image.

Two practical notes I want on the record before we commit:

- **Shell-quoting:** `:` is shell-safe bare (`--add ./my.conf:/etc/my.conf`). `->` contains `>`, which the shell treats as a redirect, so it must be quoted (`--add './bin->/usr/local/bin'`). Inside a `@file` there's no shell, so `->` is clean there. Both work; just a usability asymmetry worth knowing.
- **Dest-exists collision** is a *separate* question from the separator one: when an injected file lands on a path that already exists in the image rootfs, what should build do — **error/abort**, **overwrite**, or **skip with a warning**? My default would be overwrite (you asked for it to be there) but surface each overwrite in the resolved-plan / `--dry-run` output. Say the word if you'd rather it error.

**Is my "collision" reading right** — both separators offered as mutual escape hatches, ambiguous entries error? Or did you mean the dest-already-exists case?

One fork is still open from before, and I'll just recommend rather than re-widget it: **package layout.** I lean toward a **proper installable package** — `src/bdimager/` + `pyproject.toml` (version-of-record `0.3.0`, console scripts `bd-capture`/`bd-build`/`bd-write`). It matches versioning.md's Python case, drops fc's `sys.path`-relative imports, and gives a clean boundary for the eventual Rust port. The fc self-tests would move from `scripts/`-path `just` recipes to module/console-script invocations. Good with that, or would you rather mirror fc's flat `scripts/` + `justfile` for this first cycle?

> DEVELOPER

**Layout:** taking "a proper installable package should be handled properly" as a yes — bdimager itself becomes a proper installable Python package: `src/bdimager/` + `pyproject.toml` (version-of-record `0.3.0`), console scripts `bd-capture`/`bd-build`/`bd-write`, real package imports.

**The inject feature is layered** — this is the part I want to be sure of. You're describing two tiers for what `build` adds to the image:

- **Recognized installable package → handled "properly."** If the thing being added is a package type build understands, it gets placed/installed the right way automatically — no manual destination needed.
- **Unrecognized / plain files / a type build doesn't understand → fallback.** The user must supply the explicit destination via the `src:dest` / `src->dest` mapping (with the both-separators collision escape hatch we agreed on).

If that's right, the one scope decision left for **this** cycle is *how much* of the "proper" tier to build now. The honest engineering note: installing a real package into a mounted image rootfs (especially a foreign-arch one — a Pi SD card on an x86 box) generally needs `chroot` + `binfmt_misc`/`qemu-user`, which is a much heavier lift than fc's current rootless file-copy build.

So which of these do you want for `0.3.0`:

- **(b) Fallback + specific package handling now** — if so, tell me which package type(s) (e.g. `.deb`/dpkg, Alpine `apk`, Python wheel) and I'll fold the chroot/qemu install path into this cycle.

> AGENT

That changes the answer to your question in an important way — worth getting on the record before the ladder.

**fc's build is deliberately *not* mount-based.** To stay rootless it never loop-mounts:
- partitions are located by reading the table with `sfdisk -J`;
- the **ext4 root** is edited with `debugfs` (extract the partition to a temp file at offset 0, edit, splice back byte-for-byte);
- the **FAT boot** partition is edited with `mtools` (`image@@offset`);
- only the physical-device `dd` in capture/write needs sudo.

So, to your assumption:
- **Mountable?** Yes, the image is an ordinary partitioned disk image and *can* be loop-mounted — but mounting (losetup/mount) needs root, which is exactly what fc avoids. Our generic `--add src:dest` injection will ride the existing rootless path: `debugfs` puts the file into the ext4 root (mtools for a boot-partition dest). Rootless, no mount.
- **Will "typical" package-manager install methods work?** Not on the rootless debugfs path — that only places *files*; it can't run apt/dpkg/apk install hooks, dependency resolution, or (for a Pi/ARM image on x86) foreign binaries. Doing it "properly" needs loop-mount + `chroot` + `qemu-user`/`binfmt_misc` + root. That's precisely the heavier lift we're deferring — so the **installable-packages todo** should record "needs mount+chroot+qemu, not the rootless debugfs path." Your instinct is right; it just lives in tier-two, not the KISS fallback.

Let me confirm a couple of scaffold facts, then I'll lay out the ladder.

## Cycle 0.3.0 — Port fc's image builder into bdimager (generalized)

**Problem:** bdimager is an empty scaffold. fc has a working rootless SD-card image builder (capture → build → write, ~2,000 lines across 7 modules) that maps onto bdimager's purpose. Port it in as a proper installable package, stripped of all fc-isms, replacing fc's app-baking with a generic file/dir injection feature.

**Locked decisions:**
- Generalize, no fc-isms: `bd-config.toml`, `BD_*` env prefix, `bd-capture`/`bd-build`/`bd-write` console scripts.
- Proper installable package: `src/bdimager/` + `pyproject.toml`.
- `build` injection: explicit `src`→`dest`, accepting **both** `:` and `->` (mutual collision escape hatch); an entry ambiguous in *both* separators is a hard error; dest-already-exists → **overwrite**, surfaced in `--dry-run`/resolved plan; supplied on the CLI (`--add`) or via `@file` (argparse fromfile).
- Scope (a) KISS: file/dir copy only, on fc's rootless `debugfs`/`mtools` path — no mount. Real package *installation* deferred to a todo.

**Ladder:**

- **0.3.0-0 Preparation** — `pyproject.toml` (version-of-record `0.3.0+0`, PEP 440 `-`→`+`; hatchling; the three console scripts), `src/bdimager/` skeleton, uv/`.python-version`. Move the Rough Initial Plan into `## In Progress` (bold title + problem + this ladder); open the chores-01 section (empty `Commits:`).

- **0.3.0-1 feat: rootless storage + config core** — port `imglib`, `imgcfg`, `devsafe`, `mk_test_image` into the package, de-fc'd. `bd-config.toml` schema: drop fc build keys (`fc_path`, `fc_config_target`, `autologin_tty`, `scrub_*`), keep generic `image`/`capture`/`write`/`build` keys, add the inject key; `BD_*` env. *Validate:* `debugfs`/`mtools` round-trip smoke via `mk_test_image`.

- **0.3.0-2 feat: build app + generic file/dir injection** — port `build` (keep shrink/compress/finalize on the rootless path), remove baking/scrub logic, add the `--add`/`@file` `src:dest`/`src->dest` injection with the collision rules above. *Validate:* `image-test` self-test green, plus an inject case.

- **0.3.0-3 feat: capture + write device apps** — port `capture` + `write` de-fc'd, `--test-mode` preserved, `BD_*` config. *Validate:* `capture-test` + `write-test` self-tests green.

- **0.3.0-4 docs + deferred todo** — README per-subcommand usage (incl. `--add`/`@file` syntax + collision rules + rootless note), ARCHITECTURE module map, and the **installable-packages todo** (tier-two: needs mount+chroot+qemu-user, not the rootless debugfs path).

- **0.3.0 close-out** — full validation (all three self-tests; console scripts install + run); bookkeeping (move In Progress → chores-01 + As-built ladder, `## Done` entry, ref prune, update `notes/README.md` if needed); decide push shape at push time.

> DEVELOPER

I'd would have done capture, build then write, why build before capture?

> AGENT

Good catch — that ordering wasn't arbitrary, but it also isn't sacred. The reason build came before capture is **test dependency, not pipeline logic**:

Looking at fc's self-test recipes, both device-app tests *invoke build* as part of their end-to-end verification:
- `capture-test`: make synthetic card → **capture** → **build** → verify deploy
- `write-test`: make mother → **build** → **write** → verify expanded
- `image-test`: make mother → **build** → verify  ← build is the only one that self-tests with nothing upstream

So `build` is rootless and self-contained (mother → deploy, verifiable on its own), while `capture` and `write` can't reach a fully-green self-test until `build` exists to produce/verify the deployable image. Putting `build` first means every later step has a working validation gate — which the cycle protocol's per-step validation contract wants.

Pipeline order (capture → build → write) reads more naturally, but it costs you: the `capture` step could only do a *partial* self-test (mother integrity) until `build` lands a step later.

Two ways to go:

- **A (current):** `-2 build` → `-3 capture + write`. Each step fully self-tests when it lands.
- **B (your instinct):** `-2 capture` → `-3 build` → `-4 write`, pipeline order. `capture` gets a partial gate at `-2`, full capture→build verification at `-3`; one more work commit.

I lean A for the clean per-step gates, but it's a readability-vs-testability trade and your call. Want me to keep A, switch to B, or some hybrid (e.g. keep build first but rename the steps so the pipeline intent still reads clearly)?

> DEVELOPER

You've convinced me A. More Q's?

> AGENT

A it is — I'll update the ladder note to drop the B alternative phrasing if it crept in (it didn't; the file already reflects A).

Two real ones left, then I think I'm clear to start `0.3.0-0`:

1. **Operating mode (still open).** Does approving the ladder delegate this cycle through its commits *and pushes* unattended — I report each as it lands, and only genuinely irreversible ops (force-push over published history, history rewrite) pause for you? Or keep the normal per-push stop where I pause and ask before every push? AGENTS.md is emphatic that pushes are per-push by default and that ambiguity falls back to asking, so I want this explicit rather than inferred.

2. **Self-test harness.** fc drove its rootless self-tests as `justfile` recipes (`image-test`/`capture-test`/`write-test`). Now that bdimager is a proper package, my lean is **pytest** integration tests wrapping the same rootless flows (so `uv run pytest` is the gate, idiomatic for a package), optionally with a thin `justfile`/console convenience. Options: **pytest**, **justfile recipes (mirror fc)**, or **both**. Which?

And one assumption I'll proceed on unless you object: mirror fc's toolchain — `uv` + `hatchling`, `requires-python >=3.14`, `.python-version`. The image tools (`debugfs`/e2fsprogs, `mtools`, `sfdisk`, `xz`) are system deps, documented in README, not pip deps.

> DEVELOPER

I'll report each commit + push as it lands; only truly irreversible ops would pause.

vc-x1 0.65.2-1

Dual-repo commit+push+finalize in one resumable command.

Collapses today's manual Commit-Push-Finalize Flow into a
single subcommand with two interactive approval gates and a
state machine with persistent progress so interruptions can
resume without re-doing completed stages.

Stages: preflight (fmt/clippy/test) → review (approve diff)
→ message ($EDITOR / --title+--body, approve text) →
commit-app → commit-claude (skipped if clean) → bookmark-both
→ push-app → finalize-claude. Failures in commit-app /
commit-claude / bookmark-both roll both repos back via
`jj op restore` to the snapshot recorded before commit-app.
After push-app succeeds the remote boundary is crossed and
recovery is forward-only.

Non-interactive use: pass both --title and --body plus --yes
to skip the review gate. Saved state carries title/body
across resumes so only the first invocation needs them.

Usage: vc-x1 push [OPTIONS] [BOOKMARK]

Arguments:
  [BOOKMARK]
          Bookmark to advance in both repos (positional form of `--bookmark`).
          
          Accepting a positional lets the common case read as `vc-x1 push main`
          without the `--bookmark` ceremony; `--bookmark` is kept as an alias
          for scripts and for composition with other args. The two forms
          conflict if both supplied.

Options:
      --bookmark <BOOKMARK>
          Bookmark to advance in both repos (flag form; see positional)

  -V, --version
          Print the `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner as the first line, then continue. With
          no subcommand, prints the banner and exits.
          
          Replaces clap's auto-version (which would exit after printing): the
          banner now rides along with normal subcommand execution rather than
          gating it, so scripts can capture the version *and* the command's
          output in one invocation.

      --restart
          Clear any saved state file and start from stage 1

  -v, --verbose...
          Verbose output: -v debug, -vv trace

      --from <STAGE>
          Explicit stage to jump to (advanced / debug use)

          Possible values:
          - preflight:       Run fmt / clippy / test / install / retest
          - review:          Present diff for the first approval gate
          - message:         Compose / edit the commit message; present for
            second gate
```

```
1	"""Rootless host-side helpers for the SD-card image builder — edit the
2	partitions of a `.img` without losetup / mount / sudo.
3	
4	Mounting a `.img` needs root (loop device + mount are privileged), and
5	the secrets a deploy image must shed live on the ext4 root, so the
6	builder edits filesystems *in the file* instead:
7	
8	- partitions are located by probing the filesystem magic at each
9	  partition's byte offset (read from the MBR via `sfdisk -J`), not by
10	  trusting the MBR type code,
11	- the ext4 root is edited with `debugfs`, which has no offset option —
12	  so the root partition is extracted to a standalone temp file (a
13	  filesystem at offset 0), edited, and spliced back byte-for-byte,
14	- the FAT boot partition is edited with `mtools`, which addresses a
15	  filesystem inside an image natively via its `image@@offset` syntax.
16	
17	None of this needs root; only the physical-device `dd` of capture/write
18	does.
19	"""
20	
21	import json
22	import os
23	import subprocess
24	import tempfile
25	from pathlib import Path
26	
27	# ext4 superblock magic (0xEF53, little-endian) sits 0x438 bytes into a
28	# partition; a FAT boot sector ends with 0x55AA at offset 0x1FE. Reading
29	# these two locations identifies a partition's filesystem without root.
30	_EXT_MAGIC_OFF = 0x438
31	_EXT_MAGIC = b"\x53\xef"
32	_FAT_SIG_OFF = 0x1FE
33	_FAT_SIG = b"\x55\xaa"
34	
35	_CHUNK = 1 << 20  # 1 MiB copy buffer for extract/splice
36	
37	
38	def run(cmd: list, *, stdin: str | None = None, capture: bool = False,
39	        echo: bool = True, check: bool = True) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess:
40	    """Run a command; echo it and raise on failure by default.
41	
42	    - `stdin` feeds text to the process (used to script `sfdisk`),
43	    - `capture` returns stdout/stderr as text for callers that parse it,
44	    - `echo=False` silences the `$ cmd` line (read-only queries — lsblk,
45	      mdir, e2fsck — that shouldn't clutter a build log),
46	    - `check=False` returns a non-zero exit instead of raising (tools like
47	      e2fsck whose exit 1 means "fixed", not "failed").
48	    """
49	    if echo:
50	        print("  $", " ".join(str(c) for c in cmd))
51	    return subprocess.run(cmd, input=stdin, check=check, text=True, capture_output=capture)
52	
53	
54	def partitions(img: Path) -> list[dict]:
55	    """Return the image's partitions as dicts with byte offset and length.
56	
57	    - reads the MBR/GPT table via `sfdisk -J` (rootless),
58	    - each dict carries node, raw type, byte `off`, and byte `length`.
59	    """
60	    data = json.loads(run(["sfdisk", "-J", str(img)], capture=True).stdout)
61	    table = data["partitiontable"]
62	    sector = table.get("sectorsize", 512)
63	    out = []
64	    for p in table.get("partitions", []):
65	        out.append({
66	            "node": p.get("node"),
67	            "type": str(p.get("type", "")),
68	            "off": p["start"] * sector,
69	            "length": p["size"] * sector,
70	        })
71	    return out
72	
73	
74	def probe_fstype(img: Path, off: int) -> str | None:
75	    """Identify the filesystem at byte `off` as 'ext', 'vfat', or None."""
76	    with open(img, "rb") as f:
77	        f.seek(off + _EXT_MAGIC_OFF)
78	        if f.read(2) == _EXT_MAGIC:
79	            return "ext"
80	        f.seek(off + _FAT_SIG_OFF)
81	        if f.read(2) == _FAT_SIG:
82	            return "vfat"
83	    return None
84	
85	
86	def find_boot_root(img: Path) -> tuple[dict | None, dict | None, list[dict]]:
87	    """Return (boot_part, root_part, all_parts), each found by fs probe.
88	
89	    - boot is the first FAT partition, root the first ext partition,
90	    - each returned partition dict gains a `fstype` key.
91	    """
92	    parts = partitions(img)
93	    boot = root = None
94	    for p in parts:
95	        p["fstype"] = probe_fstype(img, p["off"])
96	        if p["fstype"] == "ext" and root is None:
97	            root = p
98	        elif p["fstype"] == "vfat" and boot is None:
99	            boot = p
100	    return boot, root, parts
101	
102	
103	def extract_region(img: Path, off: int, length: int) -> Path:
104	    """Copy `length` bytes at `off` out to a temp file; return its path.
105	
106	    The result is a standalone filesystem image (offset 0) that `debugfs`
107	    can open directly. Caller is responsible for deleting it.
108	    """
109	    fd, name = tempfile.mkstemp(prefix="fc-part-", suffix=".fs")
110	    os.close(fd)
111	    dst_path = Path(name)
112	    with open(img, "rb") as src, open(dst_path, "wb") as dst:
113	        src.seek(off)
114	        remaining = length
115	        while remaining:
116	            chunk = src.read(min(_CHUNK, remaining))
117	            if not chunk:
118	                break
119	            dst.write(chunk)
120	            remaining -= len(chunk)
121	    return dst_path
122	
123	
124	def splice_region(img: Path, off: int, src: Path) -> None:
125	    """Write `src` back into `img` starting at byte `off` (in place).
126	
127	    The source is an extracted partition edited in place, so its length
128	    is unchanged and this overwrites exactly the original region.
129	    """
130	    with open(src, "rb") as s, open(img, "r+b") as d:
131	        d.seek(off)
132	        while True:
133	            chunk = s.read(_CHUNK)
134	            if not chunk:
135	                break
136	            d.write(chunk)
137	
138	
139	def debugfs_batch(fs_file: Path, commands: list[str]) -> None:
140	    """Run a list of debugfs commands against `fs_file` in one -w session.
141	
142	    - no-op for an empty list,
143	    - commands run in order, so a `rm` then `write` then `sif` sequence
144	      (the truncate-in-place idiom) behaves as written.
145	    """
146	    if not commands:
147	        return
148	    fd, cmdfile = tempfile.mkstemp(prefix="fc-debugfs-", suffix=".txt")
149	    try:
150	        with os.fdopen(fd, "w") as f:
151	            f.write("\n".join(commands) + "\n")
152	        run(["debugfs", "-w", "-f", cmdfile, str(fs_file)], capture=True)
153	    finally:
154	        os.unlink(cmdfile)
155	
156	
157	def fs_list(fs_file: Path, path: str) -> list[dict]:
158	    """List one ext directory via `debugfs ls -l -p`; [] if absent.
159	
160	    Each entry dict carries name, inode, mode (int), uid, gid, size, and
161	    the full child path. `.` / `..` are dropped.
162	    """
163	    cp = subprocess.run(
164	        ["debugfs", "-R", f"ls -l -p {path}", str(fs_file)],
165	        capture_output=True, text=True,
166	    )
167	    entries = []
168	    for line in cp.stdout.splitlines():
169	        fields = line.split("/")
170	        # Parseable rows look like: /inode/mode/uid/gid/name/size/
171	        if len(fields) < 7:
172	            continue
173	        name = fields[5]
174	        if name in ("", ".", ".."):
175	            continue
176	        entries.append({
177	            "name": name,
178	            "inode": int(fields[1]),
179	            "mode": int(fields[2], 8) if fields[2] else 0,
180	            "uid": int(fields[3]) if fields[3] else 0,
181	            "gid": int(fields[4]) if fields[4] else 0,
182	            "size": int(fields[6]) if fields[6] else 0,
183	            "path": path.rstrip("/") + "/" + name,
184	        })
185	    return entries
186	
187	
188	def fs_walk_files(fs_file: Path, start: str):
189	    """Yield every regular-file entry under `start` (recursive).
190	
191	    - descends into subdirectories; symlinks and special files are
192	      skipped so a truncate never follows a link out of the tree.
193	    """
194	    stack = [start]
195	    while stack:
196	        current = stack.pop()
197	        for entry in fs_list(fs_file, current):
198	            kind = entry["mode"] & 0o170000
199	            if kind == 0o040000:
200	                stack.append(entry["path"])
201	            elif kind == 0o100000:
202	                yield entry
203	
204	
205	def fs_exists(fs_file: Path, path: str) -> bool:
206	    """Return True if `path` exists in the ext filesystem `fs_file`."""
207	    cp = subprocess.run(
208	        ["debugfs", "-R", f"stat {path}", str(fs_file)],
209	        capture_output=True, text=True,
210	    )
211	    return "File not found" not in (cp.stdout + cp.stderr)
212	
213	
214	def fs_read(fs_file: Path, path: str) -> str | None:
215	    """Return the text contents of `path` in ext fs `fs_file`, or None.
216	
217	    - dumps the file out with `debugfs dump` (rootless) and reads it back;
218	      None means the file is absent, distinct from an empty file (""),
219	    - used for the read-modify-write of text config the inject edits in
220	      place rather than overwrites (`/etc/inittab`, `.profile`).
221	    """
222	    fd, tmp = tempfile.mkstemp(prefix="fc-read-", suffix=".dump")
223	    os.close(fd)
224	    try:
225	        cp = subprocess.run(
226	            ["debugfs", "-R", f"dump {path} {tmp}", str(fs_file)],
227	            capture_output=True, text=True,
228	        )
229	        if "File not found" in (cp.stdout + cp.stderr):
230	            return None
231	        return Path(tmp).read_text()
232	    finally:
233	        os.unlink(tmp)
234	
235	
236	def sector_size(img: Path) -> int:
237	    """Return the image's logical sector size in bytes (from `sfdisk -J`)."""
238	    data = json.loads(run(["sfdisk", "-J", str(img)], capture=True).stdout)
239	    return data["partitiontable"].get("sectorsize", 512)
240	
241	
242	def set_part_size(img: Path, partnum: int, size_sectors: int) -> None:
243	    """Resize partition `partnum` (1-based) to `size_sectors`, keeping its start.
244	
245	    Rewrites only that table entry via `sfdisk -N`; the leading comma in the
246	    spec keeps the existing start (and type), so only the size changes.
247	    """
248	    run(["sfdisk", "--no-reread", "-N", str(partnum), str(img)],
249	        stdin=f",{size_sectors}\n", capture=True)
250	
251	
252	def ext_size(fs_file: Path) -> int:
253	    """Return the ext filesystem's size in bytes (block count * block size)."""
254	    out = subprocess.run(["dumpe2fs", "-h", str(fs_file)],
255	                         capture_output=True, text=True).stdout
256	    count = block = None
257	    for line in out.splitlines():
258	        if line.startswith("Block count:"):
259	            count = int(line.split(":", 1)[1])
260	        elif line.startswith("Block size:"):
261	            block = int(line.split(":", 1)[1])
262	    if count is None or block is None:
263	        raise SystemExit(f"imglib: could not read ext size from {fs_file}")
264	    return count * block
265	
266	
267	def e2fsck(fs_file: Path, *, fix: bool) -> int:
268	    """Run e2fsck on `fs_file`, returning its exit code (not raising).
269	
270	    - `fix=True` -> `-fy` (auto-fix, e.g. before a resize); `fix=False`
271	      -> `-fn` (check-only, e.g. a verify),
272	    - returns the code so callers can treat 0 (clean) / 1 (fixed) as ok
273	      and >=2 as a real failure.
274	    """
275	    flag = "-fy" if fix else "-fn"
276	    return run(["e2fsck", flag, str(fs_file)], capture=True, echo=False, check=False).returncode
277	
278	
279	def ext_min_resize(fs_file: Path) -> int:
280	    """Shrink ext `fs_file` to its minimum with `resize2fs -M`; return new bytes.
281	
282	    - resize2fs requires a clean fs, so `e2fsck` runs first (exit 0 =
283	      clean, 1 = fixed, both fine; >=2 is a real error),
284	    - the new size is read back from dumpe2fs after the resize.
285	    """
286	    rc = e2fsck(fs_file, fix=True)
287	    if rc >= 2:
288	        raise SystemExit(f"imglib: e2fsck failed on {fs_file} (exit {rc})")
289	    run(["resize2fs", "-M", str(fs_file)], capture=True)
290	    return ext_size(fs_file)
291	
292	
293	def ext_grow(fs_file: Path) -> int:
294	    """Grow ext `fs_file` to fill its container with `resize2fs`; return new bytes.
295	
296	    The inverse of `ext_min_resize`: `resize2fs` with no size argument
297	    expands the filesystem to fill the file (or partition). Used by the
298	    write app's expand — after the partition is grown, the rootfs is
299	    grown to fill it.
300	
301	    - resize2fs requires a clean fs, so `e2fsck` runs first (exit 0 =
302	      clean, 1 = fixed, both fine; >=2 is a real error).
303	    """
304	    rc = e2fsck(fs_file, fix=True)
305	    if rc >= 2:
306	        raise SystemExit(f"imglib: e2fsck failed on {fs_file} (exit {rc})")
307	    run(["resize2fs", str(fs_file)], capture=True)
308	    return ext_size(fs_file)
309	
310	
311	def fat_at(img: Path, off: int) -> str:
312	    """Return the mtools `image@@offset` selector for the FAT at `off`."""
313	    return f"{img}@@{off}"
314	
315	
316	def fat_list(node) -> list[str]:
317	    """Root-directory entry names of a FAT filesystem via mtools (rootless).
318	
319	    - `node` is an mtools selector — a device path (`/dev/sdb1`) or an
320	      `image@@offset` from fat_at,
321	    - the FAT counterpart of fs_list (ext via debugfs); `mdir -b` prints
322	      full paths, of which we return the basenames.
323	    """
324	    cp = run(["mdir", "-i", str(node), "-b", "::/"], capture=True, echo=False, check=False)
325	    return [os.path.basename(line.rstrip("/"))
326	            for line in cp.stdout.splitlines() if line.strip()]
327

1	"""Shared config plumbing for the SD-card image-builder apps.
2	
3	The builder is split into standalone apps — `build` (rootless),
4	`capture`, and `write` — that each own their CLI (argparse) but share how
5	settings are sourced. This module is that shared layer:
6	
7	- load the app's `fc-config.toml` tables (e.g. `[image]` + `[build]`)
8	  over a built-in default map, erroring on an unknown key,
9	- merge file < `FC_<KEY>` env < CLI flag into a `{key: (value, source)}`
10	  plan so `--dry-run` can show where each value won,
11	- generate the per-key CLI flags and print the resolved settings block.
12	
13	Settings map 1:1 to CLI flags — identical names (`scrub_logs` <->
14	`--scrub-logs`). Precedence, lowest to highest: the config tables, then
15	an `FC_<KEY>` env var, then the matching CLI flag. The config file
16	defaults to `fc-config.toml` in the project root (`..` from this
17	module's source); each app's `--config` overrides it.
18	"""
19	
20	import argparse
21	import os
22	import tomllib
23	from pathlib import Path
24	
25	# Default config: fc-config.toml in the project root, i.e. `..` from this
26	# module's source (scripts/), the same file src/fc.py reads.
27	DEFAULT_CONFIG = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / "fc-config.toml"
28	
29	# Canonical schema: every valid key in each fc-config.toml table. Used to
30	# reject typos (an unknown key in a table) while still letting an app read
31	# only the subset of a shared table it needs — e.g. capture reads [image]
32	# but not its `output`, yet `output` must not look like a typo there.
33	TABLE_KEYS = {
34	    "image": {"images_dir", "mother", "output"},
35	    "build": {
36	        "fc_path", "fc_config_target", "autologin_tty",
37	        "scrub_logs", "scrub_ssh_host_keys", "scrub_machine_id",
38	        "scrub_shell_history", "scrub_wifi", "shrink", "compress",
39	    },
40	    "capture": {"source"},
41	    "write": {"target", "target_byid", "expand", "allow_yes"},
42	}
43	
44	# FC_* env values that count as true / false (case-insensitive). Anything
45	# else is a hard error so a typo never silently flips a toggle.
46	_TRUE = {"1", "true", "yes", "on"}
47	_FALSE = {"0", "false", "no", "off"}
48	
49	
50	def parse_bool(raw: str, source: str) -> bool:
51	    """Coerce an FC_* env string to bool, erroring on anything unknown.
52	
53	    - `source` names the variable for the error message,
54	    - accepts the _TRUE / _FALSE token sets, case-insensitively.
55	    """
56	    token = raw.strip().lower()
57	    if token in _TRUE:
58	        return True
59	    if token in _FALSE:
60	        return False
61	    raise SystemExit(
62	        f"imgcfg: {source}={raw!r} is not a boolean "
63	        f"(use one of {sorted(_TRUE)} / {sorted(_FALSE)})"
64	    )
65	
66	
67	def load_config(path: Path, sections: tuple, defaults: dict) -> dict:
68	    """Return `defaults` overlaid by the named `sections` of the config.
69	
70	    - `sections` are the `fc-config.toml` tables the app reads (e.g.
71	      `("image", "build")`); later sections win on a key overlap,
72	    - a missing file is fine — fall back to `defaults` entirely so
73	      `--dry-run` works on a bare checkout,
74	    - a key not in a read section's canonical schema (`TABLE_KEYS`) is an
75	      error (a typo); a valid key the app simply doesn't resolve — e.g.
76	      capture reading [image] but not its `output` — is kept out of the
77	      plan, not flagged.
78	    """
79	    merged = dict(defaults)
80	    if not path.exists():
81	        return merged
82	    doc = tomllib.loads(path.read_text())
83	    for section in sections:
84	        table = doc.get(section, {})
85	        unknown = set(table) - TABLE_KEYS.get(section, set())
86	        if unknown:
87	            raise SystemExit(
88	                f"imgcfg: {path} [{section}] has unknown key(s): "
89	                f"{', '.join(sorted(unknown))}"
90	            )
91	        merged.update({k: v for k, v in table.items() if k in defaults})
92	    return merged
93	
94	
95	def resolve(args: argparse.Namespace, file_cfg: dict,
96	            str_keys: tuple, bool_keys: tuple) -> dict:
97	    """Merge file < FC_* env < CLI into a {key: (value, source)} plan.
98	
99	    - `source` records where the winning value came from ("config",
100	      "env", or "flag") so `--dry-run` can show the override chain,
101	    - CLI flags default to None (unset) so an absent flag falls through
102	      to env, then to the file default.
103	    """
104	    resolved: dict = {}
105	    for key in (*str_keys, *bool_keys):
106	        value = file_cfg[key]
107	        source = "config"
108	
109	        env_name = f"FC_{key.upper()}"
110	        if env_name in os.environ:
111	            raw = os.environ[env_name]
112	            value = parse_bool(raw, env_name) if key in bool_keys else raw
113	            source = "env"
114	
115	        cli = getattr(args, key)
116	        if cli is not None:
117	            value = cli
118	            source = "flag"
119	
120	        resolved[key] = (value, source)
121	    return resolved
122	
123	
124	def add_arguments(parser: argparse.ArgumentParser,
125	                  str_keys: tuple, bool_keys: tuple) -> None:
126	    """Add one config-override flag per key to `parser`.
127	
128	    - string keys take a value (`--key`); bool keys use
129	      BooleanOptionalAction so each gets a `--key` / `--no-key` pair,
130	    - every flag defaults to None (unset) so `resolve` can tell an absent
131	      flag from an explicit one and apply the precedence chain.
132	    """
133	    for key in str_keys:
134	        parser.add_argument(
135	            f"--{key.replace('_', '-')}", default=None,
136	            help=f"override config {key}",
137	        )
138	    for key in bool_keys:
139	        parser.add_argument(
140	            f"--{key.replace('_', '-')}", default=None,
141	            action=argparse.BooleanOptionalAction,
142	            help=f"override config {key}",
143	        )
144	
145	
146	def img_path(plan: dict, key: str) -> Path:
147	    """Resolve a `mother` / `output` plan value against `images_dir`.
148	
149	    A bare filename (no directory part) sits under `images_dir`; a value
150	    that already carries a path — relative or absolute — is used as-is.
151	    Shared by the apps that move these artifacts (build, capture, write).
152	    """
153	    p = Path(plan[key][0])
154	    if p.parent == Path("."):
155	        return Path(plan["images_dir"][0]) / p
156	    return p
157	
158	
159	def print_settings(config_path: Path, plan: dict,
160	                   str_keys: tuple, bool_keys: tuple, prog: str) -> None:
161	    """Print the resolved settings block: each value and where it won.
162	
163	    - the header notes whether the config file was present,
164	    - string keys show `repr`, bool keys show `on` / `off`; the `[source]`
165	      column is `config` / `env` / `flag`.
166	    """
167	    exists = "present" if config_path.exists() else "missing, using built-in defaults"
168	    print(f"{prog} plan (config: {config_path} — {exists})")
169	    print("")
170	    print("settings:")
171	    for key in (*str_keys, *bool_keys):
172	        value, source = plan[key]
173	        shown = repr(value) if key in str_keys else ("on" if value else "off")
174	        print(f"  {key:<22} {shown:<26} [{source}]")
175	
```
