1	"""Host block-device targeting + safety for the image builder's capture
2	and write — the only steps that touch a physical device (root, and
3	destructive on the wrong one).
4	
5	The targeting + safety checks are read-only and rootless (lsblk / stat
6	/ glob over /sys and /dev); the privileged `dd` writes (`dd_capture`,
7	`dd_write`) also live here — devsafe is the one module that touches a
8	physical device. The guards catch a wrong-device target before any
9	write runs:
10	
11	- two-key targeting — the caller names the device twice (`/dev/xx` and a
12	  `/dev/disk/by-id/yy`); both must resolve to the same major:minor, so a
13	  stale `/dev/sdX` enumeration can't slip through,
14	- system-disk refusal — refuse any target whose lsblk subtree backs a
15	  mounted filesystem, walking the dm / LVM / md stack (root-on-LVM-on-
16	  RAID-on-NVMe, not just a direct mount),
17	- content preview — list each partition's root rootlessly (debugfs for
18	  ext, mtools for FAT) so the human sees what they'd read or destroy,
19	- confirmation — interactive, bypassable only via fc-config.toml.
20	"""
21	
22	import json
23	import os
24	import shutil
25	import stat as statmod
26	from pathlib import Path
27	
28	import imglib
29	
30	BY_ID = Path("/dev/disk/by-id")
31	
32	# Top-level directory names that mark a partition as a real OS / data
33	# filesystem; surfaced as an extra warning in the confirm summary.
34	_SYSTEM_MARKERS = {"etc", "boot", "home", "bin", "usr", "var", "root", "lib"}
35	
36	
37	def _lsblk(cols: str) -> list[dict]:
38	    """Return `lsblk -J` (tree) for the given columns, parsed to blockdevices.
39	
40	    NAME is always requested even if the caller doesn't use it: lsblk only
41	    nests `children` when NAME is among the output columns, and the
42	    safety walks (subtree mount checks, partition listing) depend on that
43	    tree — without it the list is flat and the walks silently see nothing.
44	    """
45	    cp = imglib.run(["lsblk", "-J", "-o", f"NAME,{cols}"], capture=True, echo=False)
46	    return json.loads(cp.stdout).get("blockdevices", [])
47	
48	
49	def majmin(path: str) -> str:
50	    """Return 'major:minor' of the block device at `path` (follows symlinks)."""
51	    st = os.stat(path)
52	    if not statmod.S_ISBLK(st.st_mode):
53	        raise SystemExit(f"devsafe: {path} is not a block device")
54	    return f"{os.major(st.st_rdev)}:{os.minor(st.st_rdev)}"
55	
56	
57	def verify_two_key(dev_path: str, byid_path: str) -> str:
58	    """Confirm both targets name the same device; return the resolved /dev path.
59	
60	    - `byid_path` must live under /dev/disk/by-id/ (the stable, hardware
61	      handle); a by-uuid or bare name is rejected,
62	    - both must resolve to the same major:minor, else abort.
63	    """
64	    if BY_ID not in Path(byid_path).parents:
65	        raise SystemExit(
66	            f"devsafe: second target must be a {BY_ID}/ path, got {byid_path}")
67	    a, b = majmin(dev_path), majmin(byid_path)
68	    if a != b:
69	        raise SystemExit(
70	            f"devsafe: targets disagree — {dev_path}={a} vs {byid_path}={b}; aborting")
71	    return os.path.realpath(dev_path)
72	
73	
74	def resolve_device(path: str, *, test_mode: bool = False) -> str:
75	    """Resolve a single-device handle to its canonical path.
76	
77	    - accepts either form — a `/dev/xx` node or a `/dev/disk/by-id/yy`
78	      symlink — and returns `os.path.realpath(path)`,
79	    - asserts the result is a block device, except in `test_mode` where a
80	      regular file stands in for a card (so capture is self-testable with
81	      no root / hardware).
82	
83	    This is the single-key resolution capture uses (a read needs no
84	    cross-check); `verify_two_key` layers the two-key major:minor match
85	    on top for the destructive write.
86	    """
87	    real = os.path.realpath(path)
88	    if test_mode:
89	        return real
90	    if not Path(real).exists():
91	        raise SystemExit(f"devsafe: {path} does not exist")
92	    if not statmod.S_ISBLK(os.stat(real).st_mode):
93	        raise SystemExit(f"devsafe: {path} ({real}) is not a block device")
94	    return real
95	
96	
97	def dd_capture(src_dev: str, dest_img: Path, *, test_mode: bool = False) -> None:
98	    """Copy a source block device into the mother image — the capture write.
99	
100	    - real mode: `sudo dd if=<src_dev> of=<dest_img>` — root, since
101	      reading a block device needs it; `conv=fsync` flushes before return,
102	    - `test_mode`: a rootless file copy, so the capture path runs against
103	      a synthetic mk_test_image `.img` standing in for a device,
104	    - the destination's parent is created and any existing dest removed
105	      first, so a smaller capture never leaves a stale tail.
106	    """
107	    dest_img = Path(dest_img)
108	    dest_img.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
109	    dest_img.unlink(missing_ok=True)
110	    if test_mode:
111	        shutil.copyfile(src_dev, dest_img)
112	        print(f"  capture: copied {src_dev} -> {dest_img} (test-mode)")
113	        return
114	    imglib.run(["sudo", "dd", f"if={src_dev}", f"of={dest_img}",
115	                "bs=4M", "status=progress", "conv=fsync"])
116	
117	
118	def dd_write(src_img: Path, dest_dev: str, *, test_mode: bool = False) -> None:
119	    """Write the deployable image to a target device — the write app's dd.
120	
121	    - real mode: `sudo dd if=<src_img> of=<dest_dev>` — root, since
122	      writing a block device needs it; `conv=fsync` flushes before return,
123	    - `test_mode`: write the image bytes into `dest_dev` as a file *without
124	      truncating*, so a pre-sized "card" file keeps its (larger) size for
125	      the expand step to grow into; an absent dest is created at image
126	      size,
127	    - unlike a device, a file's tail past the image is preserved — that's
128	      deliberate, the expand step fills it.
129	    """
130	    if test_mode:
131	        data = Path(src_img).read_bytes()
132	        mode = "r+b" if Path(dest_dev).exists() else "wb"
133	        with open(dest_dev, mode) as f:
134	            f.write(data)
135	        print(f"  write: wrote {src_img} -> {dest_dev} (test-mode)")
136	        return
137	    imglib.run(["sudo", "dd", f"if={src_img}", f"of={dest_dev}",
138	                "bs=4M", "status=progress", "conv=fsync"])
139	
140	
141	def expand_to_device(dev: str, *, test_mode: bool = False) -> None:
142	    """Grow the root partition + filesystem to fill the device — write's expand.
143	
144	    After the (shrunk) image is written, the device is larger than it, so
145	    the last partition and its ext rootfs are grown to reclaim the free
146	    space:
147	
148	    - real mode: `sudo sfdisk` extends the last partition, then `sudo
149	      resize2fs` grows the live filesystem to fill it (validated on
150	      hardware at the 0.3.0 close-out),
151	    - `test_mode`: the device is a file, so the rootless imglib path —
152	      grow the partition-table entry, then extract -> `resize2fs` ->
153	      splice the root region (finalize's shrink, in reverse).
154	    """
155	    if test_mode:
156	        _expand_file(Path(dev))
157	    else:
158	        _expand_device(dev)
159	
160	
161	def _expand_file(card: Path) -> None:
162	    """Rootless expand of a file: grow the root partition + rootfs to fill it."""
163	    _boot, root, parts = imglib.find_boot_root(card)
164	    if root is None:
165	        raise SystemExit(f"devsafe: no ext root partition in {card}")
166	    partnum = parts.index(root) + 1
167	    sector = imglib.sector_size(card)
168	    card_size = card.stat().st_size
169	    # Sector-align the new size so the partition entry and the extracted
170	    # region agree — otherwise the grown fs could spill past the partition.
171	    new_sectors = (card_size - root["off"]) // sector
172	    part_bytes = new_sectors * sector
173	    if part_bytes <= root["length"]:
174	        print(f"  expand: root already fills {card}; nothing to do")
175	        return
176	    print(f"\nexpand: root partition #{partnum} -> fill {card_size} B (test-mode)")
177	    # 1. grow the partition-table entry to span to the end of the card
178	    imglib.set_part_size(card, partnum, new_sectors)
179	    # 2. grow the rootfs to fill the enlarged partition (extract the now
180	    #    larger region — old fs + zero pad — resize2fs to fill, splice back)
181	    root_fs = imglib.extract_region(card, root["off"], part_bytes)
182	    try:
183	        grown = imglib.ext_grow(root_fs)
184	        imglib.splice_region(card, root["off"], root_fs)
185	    finally:
186	        root_fs.unlink(missing_ok=True)
187	    print(f"  expand: root partition -> {part_bytes} B, rootfs -> {grown} B")
188	
189	
190	def _expand_device(dev: str) -> None:
191	    """Privileged expand of a block device: sudo sfdisk grow + sudo resize2fs.
192	
193	    Untested in CI (needs a real card + root); validated on hardware at
194	    the 0.3.0 close-out. The partition node is `<dev>p<n>` for nvme/mmc
195	    (the device name ends in a digit), else `<dev><n>`.
196	    """
197	    _boot, root, parts = imglib.find_boot_root(Path(dev))
198	    if root is None:
199	        raise SystemExit(f"devsafe: no ext root partition on {dev}")
200	    partnum = parts.index(root) + 1
201	    part_node = f"{dev}{'p' if dev[-1].isdigit() else ''}{partnum}"
202	    print(f"\nexpand: grow {dev} partition #{partnum} to fill, then resize2fs")
203	    # 1. extend the last partition to fill the device (",+" keeps the
204	    #    start, size = all remaining space), then re-read the table
205	    imglib.run(["sudo", "sfdisk", "--no-reread", "-N", str(partnum), dev],
206	               stdin=",+\n")
207	    imglib.run(["sudo", "partprobe", dev])
208	    # 2. grow the live filesystem to fill the partition
209	    imglib.run(["sudo", "e2fsck", "-fy", part_node], check=False)
210	    imglib.run(["sudo", "resize2fs", part_node])
211	
212	
213	def _aliases(directory: Path) -> dict:
214	    """Map each block device's kernel name -> sorted alias names under `directory`."""
215	    out: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
216	    if not directory.is_dir():
217	        return out
218	    for link in directory.iterdir():
219	        try:
220	            kname = os.path.basename(os.path.realpath(link))
221	        except OSError:
222	            continue
223	        out.setdefault(kname, []).append(link.name)
224	    for names in out.values():
225	        names.sort()
226	    return out
227	
228	
229	def _subtree_mounted(node: dict) -> bool:
230	    """True if `node` or any descendant has a non-empty mountpoint."""
231	    if any(m for m in (node.get("mountpoints") or [])):
232	        return True
233	    return any(_subtree_mounted(c) for c in node.get("children", []))
234	
235	
236	def protected_knames() -> set:
237	    """Whole-disk kernel names that back any mounted filesystem.
238	
239	    A disk is protected if any node in its lsblk subtree (its partitions,
240	    and any dm / LVM / md / swap layered on them) is mounted — so a root
241	    on LVM-on-RAID-on-NVMe protects every underlying disk, not just a
242	    device with a direct mountpoint.
243	    """
244	    return {d["kname"] for d in _lsblk("KNAME,TYPE,MOUNTPOINTS")
245	            if d.get("type") == "disk" and _subtree_mounted(d)}
246	
247	
248	def assert_safe_target(device: str) -> None:
249	    """Abort if `device` (resolved whole disk) backs a mounted filesystem.
250	
251	    The decisive guard — refuse the system / in-use disk. There is no
252	    override: a mounted-stack disk is never a capture/write target.
253	    """
254	    kname = os.path.basename(os.path.realpath(device))
255	    if kname in protected_knames():
256	        raise SystemExit(
257	            f"devsafe: REFUSING {device} ({kname}) — it backs a mounted "
258	            "filesystem (system / in-use disk), never a valid target.")
259	
260	
261	def _device_partitions(device: str) -> list[dict]:
262	    """Return the partition child nodes (kname + fstype) of `device`."""
263	    kname = os.path.basename(os.path.realpath(device))
264	    for d in _lsblk("KNAME,FSTYPE,TYPE"):
265	        if d["kname"] == kname:
266	            return [c for c in d.get("children", []) if c.get("type") == "part"]
267	    return []
268	
269	
270	def preview_partitions(device: str) -> list[dict]:
271	    """List each partition of `device` with its fs type and root entries.
272	
273	    Rootless content peek (debugfs for ext, mtools for FAT) so the human
274	    sees what's on the device before a destructive write; each entry
275	    flags top-level system markers (etc/, boot/, home/, ...).
276	    """
277	    out = []
278	    for p in _device_partitions(device):
279	        node = f"/dev/{p['kname']}"
280	        fstype = (p.get("fstype") or "").lower()
281	        if fstype.startswith("ext"):
282	            names = [e["name"] for e in imglib.fs_list(Path(node), "/")]
283	        elif fstype in ("vfat", "fat", "msdos"):
284	            names = imglib.fat_list(node)
285	        else:
286	            names = []
287	        system = sorted({n.lower() for n in names} & _SYSTEM_MARKERS)
288	        out.append({"node": node, "fstype": fstype or "?",
289	                    "entries": names, "system": system})
290	    return out
291	
292	
293	def confirm(device: str, action: str, *, allow_yes: bool, yes_flag: bool) -> None:
294	    """Show the target summary and require confirmation for a device write.
295	
296	    - prints the device and each partition's root preview, flagging any
297	      that look like a system / data filesystem,
298	    - the prompt is skipped only when BOTH `allow_yes` (fc-config.toml,
299	      the persistent permission) and `yes_flag` (the per-run `--yes`) are
300	      set — neither alone suffices,
301	    - `--yes` without `allow_yes` is a hard error (checked first), so a
302	      misconfigured skip fails loud instead of silently dropping to a
303	      prompt that might be answered on autopilot.
304	    """
305	    if yes_flag and not allow_yes:
306	        raise SystemExit(
307	            "devsafe: --yes requires [image].allow_yes = true in fc-config.toml")
308	    print(f"\n=== {action}: {device} ===")
309	    for p in preview_partitions(device):
310	        warn = f"   [SYSTEM: {', '.join(p['system'])}]" if p["system"] else ""
311	        listing = ", ".join(p["entries"]) or "(empty)"
312	        print(f"  {p['node']} ({p['fstype']}): {listing}{warn}")
313	    if allow_yes and yes_flag:
314	        print("devsafe: allow_yes + --yes set — skipping prompt.")
315	        return
316	    reply = input(f"\nProceed to {action} {device}? type 'yes' to confirm: ").strip()
317	    if reply != "yes":
318	        raise SystemExit("devsafe: not confirmed; aborting.")
319	
320	
321	def list_devices() -> int:
322	    """Print whole-disk block devices with size / bus / model / by-id / mounts.
323	
324	    The helper for picking a capture/write target: shows each disk's
325	    by-id alias (the handle to pass as the second target) and flags any
326	    that back a mount (the system disk, which would be refused).
327	    """
328	    byid = _aliases(BY_ID)
329	    protected = protected_knames()
330	    print("block devices (whole disks):\n")
331	    for d in _lsblk("KNAME,SIZE,TRAN,MODEL,TYPE"):
332	        if d.get("type") != "disk":
333	            continue
334	        kname = d["kname"]
335	        flag = "   [SYSTEM/MOUNTED — would be refused]" if kname in protected else ""
336	        print(f"  /dev/{kname}  {d.get('size', '?'):>9}  "
337	              f"{(d.get('tran') or '-'):<5}  {d.get('model') or '-'}{flag}")
338	        aliases = byid.get(kname)
339	        if aliases:
340	            for alias in aliases:
341	                print(f"      by-id: /dev/disk/by-id/{alias}")
342	        else:
343	            print("      by-id: (none — udev/mdev may not cover this device)")
344	    return 0
345

1	"""Build / verify a small synthetic Pi-like `.img` for testing
2	build.py's offline sanitize + inject + finalize — rootless, stdlib + imglib only.
3	
4	A real mother image is multi-GB and only exists after a card capture.
5	To exercise the sanitize + inject passes on their own, this helper makes
6	a ~160 MB image with the same shape as a Pi card — a FAT boot partition
7	plus an ext4 root — seeded with exactly the files those passes target
8	(`scrub_*` victims and the inject targets). `verify` reports the outcome.
9	
10	Like the sanitize itself, it needs no root: `sfdisk` writes the table,
11	`mke2fs` + `debugfs` build and seed a standalone ext4 root that is then
12	spliced into the image, and `mtools` formats + seeds the FAT boot via
13	its `image@@offset` syntax.
14	
15	    python3 scripts/mk_test_image.py build images/mother.img
16	    python3 scripts/build.py --mother images/mother.img \\
17	        --output images/fc-deploy.img --shrink --compress
18	    python3 scripts/mk_test_image.py verify images/fc-deploy.img
19	"""

21	import argparse
22	import subprocess
23	import tempfile
24	from pathlib import Path
25	
26	import imglib

28	TOTAL_BYTES = 160 * 1024 * 1024  # 160 MB: FAT32 boot (~47 MB) + ext4 root

30	# DOS table: ~47 MB FAT boot (type c) then the rest ext4 (type 83),
31	# matching a Pi card's p1/p2 layout.
32	SFDISK_SCRIPT = """label: dos
33	unit: sectors
34	start=2048, size=96256, type=c
35	type=83
36	"""
37	
38	# Files the scrub passes target, seeded on the ext4 root so a
39	# post-sanitize verify can confirm each is gone / emptied. Paths are
40	# absolute within the filesystem.
41	SEED_ROOT = {
42	    "/etc/ssh/ssh_host_rsa_key": "PRIVATE RSA KEY\n",
43	    "/etc/ssh/ssh_host_rsa_key.pub": "ssh-rsa AAAA...\n",
44	    "/etc/ssh/ssh_host_ed25519_key": "PRIVATE ED25519 KEY\n",
45	    "/etc/ssh/ssh_host_ed25519_key.pub": "ssh-ed25519 AAAA...\n",
46	    "/var/log/syslog": "boot messages\n" * 50,
47	    "/var/log/auth.log": "login records\n" * 50,
48	    "/var/log/nginx/access.log": "GET / 200\n" * 50,
49	    "/etc/machine-id": "0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef\n",
50	    "/var/lib/dbus/machine-id": "0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef\n",

52	    "/home/pi/.bash_history": "vcgencmd measure_temp\n",
53	    "/home/pi/.python_history": "print('hi')\n",

55	    "/etc/NetworkManager/system-connections/MyWifi.nmconnection": "[wifi]\nssid=home\n",

57	    #   - a stale deploy config that must be overwritten,

59	    #     autologin (tty2's line must survive untouched),
60	    #   - an existing .profile whose content must be preserved with the
61	    #     fc launch appended,
62	    #   - an interim local.d autostart from an older build, to be removed.
63	    "/home/alpine/data/prgs/nps-gnc/fc/fc-config.toml": "OLD STALE DEPLOY CONFIG\n",

77	    "cmdline.txt": "console=serial0,115200 root=/dev/mmcblk0p2\n",

96	
97	def pick_boot_root(img: Path) -> tuple[dict, dict]:
98	    """Return (boot, root) partition dicts by MBR type (FAT 'c', ext '83').
99	
100	    Used at build time, before any filesystem exists to probe — so it
101	    keys off the partition table type, not a filesystem magic.
102	    """
103	    fat = {"1", "4", "6", "b", "c", "e", "ef"}
104	    parts = imglib.partitions(img)
105	    boot = next(p for p in parts if p["type"].lower().lstrip("0") in fat)
106	    root = next(p for p in parts if p["type"] == "83")
107	    return boot, root
108	
109	
110	def seed_root(root_fs: Path) -> None:
111	    """mke2fs has run on `root_fs`; seed it with the SEED_ROOT files.
112	
113	    Stages each file's content to a host temp file, then drives one
114	    `debugfs -w` batch of mkdir + write commands. debugfs mkdir is not
115	    recursive, so every ancestor directory is created, shallowest first.
116	    """
117	    dirs: set[str] = set()
118	    for path in SEED_ROOT:
119	        parent = Path(path).parent
120	        while str(parent) != "/":
121	            dirs.add(str(parent))
122	            parent = parent.parent
123	    ordered = sorted(dirs, key=lambda d: d.count("/"))
124	
125	    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix="mk-seed-") as tmp:
126	        cmds = [f"mkdir {d}" for d in ordered]
127	        for i, (path, content) in enumerate(SEED_ROOT.items()):
128	            host = Path(tmp) / f"seed{i}"
129	            host.write_text(content)
130	            cmds.append(f"write {host} {path}")
131	        imglib.debugfs_batch(root_fs, cmds)
132	
133	
134	def build(img: Path) -> int:
135	    """Create a seeded synthetic Pi-like image at `img` (overwrites)."""
136	    img.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
137	    with open(img, "wb") as f:
138	        f.truncate(TOTAL_BYTES)
139	    imglib.run(["sfdisk", str(img)], stdin=SFDISK_SCRIPT, capture=True)
140	
141	    boot, root = pick_boot_root(img)
142	
143	    # Build + seed a standalone ext4 root, then splice it into the image.
144	    root_fs = Path(tempfile.mkstemp(prefix="mk-root-", suffix=".ext4")[1])
145	    try:
146	        with open(root_fs, "wb") as f:
147	            f.truncate(root["length"])
148	        imglib.run(["mke2fs", "-F", "-q", "-t", "ext4", str(root_fs)], capture=True)
149	        seed_root(root_fs)
150	        imglib.splice_region(img, root["off"], root_fs)
151	    finally:
152	        root_fs.unlink(missing_ok=True)
153	
154	    # Format + seed the FAT boot partition in place via mtools @@offset.
155	    selector = imglib.fat_at(img, boot["off"])
156	    imglib.run(["mformat", "-i", selector, "-F", "::"], capture=True)
157	    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix="mk-boot-") as tmp:
158	        for name, content in SEED_BOOT.items():
159	            host = Path(tmp) / name
160	            host.write_text(content)
161	            imglib.run(
162	                ["mcopy", "-i", selector, "-o", str(host), f"::{name}"], capture=True
163	            )
164	
165	    print(
166	        f"mk_test_image: built seeded image {img} ({TOTAL_BYTES // (1024 * 1024)} MB)"
167	    )
168	    return 0

175	        if e["name"] == name:

277	def verify_expanded(img: Path) -> int:
278	    """Check an expanded card: root partition + rootfs grew to fill it.
279	
280	    Asserts the write app's expand worked — the last partition reaches
281	    (within a sector of) the end of the card, the ext rootfs fills that
282	    partition, e2fsck is clean, and the injected config survived the grow.
283	    """
284	    _boot, root, _parts = imglib.find_boot_root(img)
285	    if root is None:
286	        raise SystemExit(f"mk_test_image: no ext root partition in {img}")
287	    img_size = img.stat().st_size
288	    sector = imglib.sector_size(img)
289	    failures = []
290	
291	    print("verify expand:")
292	    root_end = root["off"] + root["length"]
293	    ok = root_end >= img_size - sector
294	    print(f"  {'PASS' if ok else 'FAIL'} partition fills card "
295	          f"(root end {root_end} B vs card {img_size} B)")
296	    if not ok:
297	        failures.append("partition-fill")
298	
299	    root_fs = imglib.extract_region(img, root["off"], root["length"])
300	    try:
301	        rc = imglib.e2fsck(root_fs, fix=False)
302	        ok = rc == 0
303	        print(f"  {'PASS' if ok else 'FAIL'} e2fsck clean (exit {rc})")
304	        if not ok:
305	            failures.append("e2fsck")
306	        fs_size = imglib.ext_size(root_fs)
307	        ok = fs_size >= root["length"] - 1024 * 1024
308	        print(f"  {'PASS' if ok else 'FAIL'} rootfs fills partition "
309	              f"(fs {fs_size} B vs part {root['length']} B)")
310	        if not ok:
311	            failures.append("fs-fill")
312	        cfg = imglib.fs_read(root_fs, "/home/alpine/data/prgs/nps-gnc/fc/fc-config.toml")
313	        ok = cfg is not None
314	        print(f"  {'PASS' if ok else 'FAIL'} inject survived (config present)")
315	        if not ok:
316	            failures.append("inject")
317	    finally:
318	        root_fs.unlink(missing_ok=True)
319	
320	    if failures:
321	        print(f"\nmk_test_image: expand verify — {len(failures)} check(s) FAILED")
322	        return 1
323	    print("\nmk_test_image: expand verify all passed")
324	    return 0
325	
326	
327	def main() -> int:
328	    """Parse the build/verify/verify-expanded subcommand and dispatch (rootless)."""
329	    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(
330	        prog="mk_test_image", description=__doc__.splitlines()[0]
331	    )
332	    sub = p.add_subparsers(dest="cmd", required=True)
333	    for name in ("build", "verify", "verify-expanded"):
334	        sub.add_parser(name).add_argument("img", type=Path)
335	    args = p.parse_args()
336	    if args.cmd == "build":
337	        return build(args.img)
338	    if args.cmd == "verify":
339	        return verify(args.img)
340	    return verify_expanded(args.img)
341	
342	
343	if __name__ == "__main__":
344	    raise SystemExit(main())
345

  "content": "\"\"\"Rootless host-side helpers for the block-device image builder — edit\nthe partitions of a `.img` without losetup / mount / sudo.\n\nMounting a `.img` needs root (loop device + mount are privileged), so\nthe builder edits filesystems *in the file* instead:\n\n- partitions are located by probing the filesystem magic at each\n  partition's byte offset (read from the MBR via `sfdisk -J`), not by\n  trusting the MBR type code,\n- the ext4 root is edited with `debugfs`, which has no offset option —\n  so the root partition is extracted to a standalone temp file (a\n  filesystem at offset 0), edited, and spliced back byte-for-byte,\n- the FAT boot partition is edited with `mtools`, which addresses a\n  filesystem inside an image natively via its `image@@offset` syntax.\n\nNone of this needs root; only the physical-device `dd` of capture/write\ndoes.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nimport os\nimport subprocess\nimport tempfile\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\n# ext4 superblock magic (0xEF53, little-endian) sits 0x438 bytes into a\n# partition; a FAT boot sector ends with 0x55AA at offset 0x1FE. Reading\n# these two locations identifies a partition's filesystem without root.\n_EXT_MAGIC_OFF = 0x438\n_EXT_MAGIC = b\"\\x53\\xef\"\n_FAT_SIG_OFF = 0x1FE\n_FAT_SIG = b\"\\x55\\xaa\"\n\n_CHUNK = 1 << 20  # 1 MiB copy buffer for extract/splice\n\n\ndef run(cmd: list, *, stdin: str | None = None, capture: bool = False,\n        echo: bool = True, check: bool = True) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess:\n    \"\"\"Run a command; echo it and raise on failure by default.\n\n    - `stdin` feeds text to the process (used to script `sfdisk`),\n    - `capture` returns stdout/stderr as text for callers that parse it,\n    - `echo=False` silences the `$ cmd` line (read-only queries — lsblk,\n      mdir, e2fsck — that shouldn't clutter a build log),\n    - `check=False` returns a non-zero exit instead of raising (tools like\n      e2fsck whose exit 1 means \"fixed\", not \"failed\").\n    \"\"\"\n    if echo:\n        print(\"  $\", \" \".join(str(c) for c in cmd))\n    return subprocess.run(cmd, input=stdin, check=check, text=True, capture_output=capture)\n\n\ndef partitions(img: Path) -> list[dict]:\n    \"\"\"Return the image's partitions as dicts with byte offset and length.\n\n    - reads the MBR/GPT table via `sfdisk -J` (rootless),\n    - each dict carries node, raw type, byte `off`, and byte `length`.\n    \"\"\"\n    data = json.loads(run([\"sfdisk\", \"-J\", str(img)], capture=True).stdout)\n    table = data[\"partitiontable\"]\n    sector = table.get(\"sectorsize\", 512)\n    out = []\n    for p in table.get(\"partitions\", []):\n        out.append({\n            \"node\": p.get(\"node\"),\n            \"type\": str(p.get(\"type\", \"\")),\n            \"off\": p[\"start\"] * sector,\n            \"length\": p[\"size\"] * sector,\n        })\n    return out\n\n\ndef probe_fstype(img: Path, off: int) -> str | None:\n    \"\"\"Identify the filesystem at byte `off` as 'ext', 'vfat', or None.\"\"\"\n    with open(img, \"rb\") as f:\n        f.seek(off + _EXT_MAGIC_OFF)\n        if f.read(2) == _EXT_MAGIC:\n            return \"ext\"\n        f.seek(off + _FAT_SIG_OFF)\n        if f.read(2) == _FAT_SIG:\n            return \"vfat\"\n    return None\n\n\ndef find_boot_root(img: Path) -> tuple[dict | None, dict | None, list[dict]]:\n    \"\"\"Return (boot_part, root_part, all_parts), each found by fs probe.\n\n    - boot is the first FAT partition, root the first ext partition,\n    - each returned partition dict gains a `fstype` key.\n    \"\"\"\n    parts = partitions(img)\n    boot = root = None\n    for p in parts:\n        p[\"fstype\"] = probe_fstype(img, p[\"off\"])\n        if p[\"fstype\"] == \"ext\" and root is None:\n            root = p\n        elif p[\"fstype\"] == \"vfat\" and boot is None:\n            boot = p\n    return boot, root, parts\n\n\ndef extract_region(img: Path, off: int, length: int) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Copy `length` bytes at `off` out to a temp file; return its path.\n\n    The result is a standalone filesystem image (offset 0) that `debugfs`\n    can open directly. Caller is responsible for deleting it.\n    \"\"\"\n    fd, name = tempfile.mkstemp(prefix=\"bd-part-\", suffix=\".fs\")\n    os.close(fd)\n    dst_path = Path(name)\n    with open(img, \"rb\") as src, open(dst_path, \"wb\") as dst:\n        src.seek(off)\n        remaining = length\n        while remaining:\n            chunk = src.read(min(_CHUNK, remaining))\n            if not chunk:\n                break\n            dst.write(chunk)\n            remaining -= len(chunk)\n    return dst_path\n\n\ndef splice_region(img: Path, off: int, src: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Write `src` back into `img` starting at byte `off` (in place).\n\n    The source is an extracted partition edited in place, so its length\n    is unchanged and this overwrites exactly the original region.\n    \"\"\"\n    with open(src, \"rb\") as s, open(img, \"r+b\") as d:\n        d.seek(off)\n        while True:\n            chunk = s.read(_CHUNK)\n            if not chunk:\n                break\n            d.write(chunk)\n\n\ndef debugfs_batch(fs_file: Path, commands: list[str]) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Run a list of debugfs commands against `fs_file` in one -w session.\n\n    - no-op for an empty list,\n    - commands run in order, so a `rm` then `write` then `sif` sequence\n      (the truncate-in-place idiom) behaves as written.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not commands:\n        return\n    fd, cmdfile = tempfile.mkstemp(prefix=\"bd-debugfs-\", suffix=\".txt\")\n    try:\n        with os.fdopen(fd, \"w\") as f:\n            f.write(\"\\n\".join(commands) + \"\\n\")\n        run([\"debugfs\", \"-w\", \"-f\", cmdfile, str(fs_file)], capture=True)\n    finally:\n        os.unlink(cmdfile)\n\n\ndef fs_list(fs_file: Path, path: str) -> list[dict]:\n    \"\"\"List one ext directory via `debugfs ls -l -p`; [] if absent.\n\n    Each entry dict carries name, inode, mode (int), uid, gid, size, and\n    the full child path. `.` / `..` are dropped.\n    \"\"\"\n    cp = subprocess.run(\n        [\"debugfs\", \"-R\", f\"ls -l -p {path}\", str(fs_file)],\n        capture_output=True, text=True,\n    )\n    entries = []\n    for line in cp.stdout.splitlines():\n        fields = line.split(\"/\")\n        # Parseable rows look like: /inode/mode/uid/gid/name/size/\n        if len(fields) < 7:\n            continue\n        name = fields[5]\n        if name in (\"\", \".\", \"..\"):\n            continue\n        entries.append({\n            \"name\": name,\n            \"inode\": int(fields[1]),\n            \"mode\": int(fields[2], 8) if fields[2] else 0,\n            \"uid\": int(fields[3]) if fields[3] else 0,\n            \"gid\": int(fields[4]) if fields[4] else 0,\n            \"size\": int(fields[6]) if fields[6] else 0,\n            \"path\": path.rstrip(\"/\") + \"/\" + name,\n        })\n    return entries\n\n\ndef fs_walk_files(fs_file: Path, start: str):\n    \"\"\"Yield every regular-file entry under `start` (recursive).\n\n    - descends into subdirectories; symlinks and special files are\n      skipped so a truncate never follows a link out of the tree.\n    \"\"\"\n    stack = [start]\n    while stack:\n        current = stack.pop()\n        for entry in fs_list(fs_file, current):\n            kind = entry[\"mode\"] & 0o170000\n            if kind == 0o040000:\n                stack.append(entry[\"path\"])\n            elif kind == 0o100000:\n                yield entry\n\n\ndef fs_exists(fs_file: Path, path: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Return True if `path` exists in the ext filesystem `fs_file`.\"\"\"\n    cp = subprocess.run(\n        [\"debugfs\", \"-R\", f\"stat {path}\", str(fs_file)],\n        capture_output=True, text=True,\n    )\n    return \"File not found\" not in (cp.stdout + cp.stderr)\n\n\ndef fs_read(fs_file: Path, path: str) -> str | None:\n    \"\"\"Return the text contents of `path` in ext fs `fs_file`, or None.\n\n    - dumps the file out with `debugfs dump` (rootless) and reads it back;\n      None means the file is absent, distinct from an empty file (\"\"),\n    - used for the read-modify-write of text content an edit changes in\n      place rather than overwriting.\n    \"\"\"\n    fd, tmp = tempfile.mkstemp(prefix=\"bd-read-\", suffix=\".dump\")\n    os.close(fd)\n    try:\n        cp = subprocess.run(\n            [\"debugfs\", \"-R\", f\"dump {path} {tmp}\", str(fs_file)],\n            capture_output=True, text=True,\n        )\n        if \"File not found\" in (cp.stdout + cp.stderr):\n            return None\n        return Path(tmp).read_text()\n    finally:\n        os.unlink(tmp)\n\n\ndef sector_size(img: Path) -> int:\n    \"\"\"Return the image's logical sector size in bytes (from `sfdisk -J`).\"\"\"\n    data = json.loads(run([\"sfdisk\", \"-J\", str(img)], capture=True).stdout)\n    return data[\"partitiontable\"].get(\"sectorsize\", 512)\n\n\ndef set_part_size(img: Path, partnum: int, size_sectors: int) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Resize partition `partnum` (1-based) to `size_sectors`, keeping its start.\n\n    Rewrites only that table entry via `sfdisk -N`; the leading comma in the\n    spec keeps the existing start (and type), so only the size changes.\n    \"\"\"\n    run([\"sfdisk\", \"--no-reread\", \"-N\", str(partnum), str(img)],\n        stdin=f\",{size_sectors}\\n\", capture=True)\n\n\ndef ext_size(fs_file: Path) -> int:\n    \"\"\"Return the ext filesystem's size in bytes (block count * block size).\"\"\"\n    out = subprocess.run([\"dumpe2fs\", \"-h\", str(fs_file)],\n                         capture_output=True, text=True).stdout\n    count = block = None\n    for line in out.splitlines():\n        if line.startswith(\"Block count:\"):\n            count = int(line.split(\":\", 1)[1])\n        elif line.startswith(\"Block size:\"):\n            block = int(line.split(\":\", 1)[1])\n    if count is None or block is None:\n        raise SystemExit(f\"imglib: could not read ext size from {fs_file}\")\n    return count * block\n\n\ndef e2fsck(fs_file: Path, *, fix: bool) -> int:\n    \"\"\"Run e2fsck on `fs_file`, returning its exit code (not raising).\n\n    - `fix=True` -> `-fy` (auto-fix, e.g. before a resize); `fix=False`\n      -> `-fn` (check-only, e.g. a verify),\n    - returns the code so callers can treat 0 (clean) / 1 (fixed) as ok\n      and >=2 as a real failure.\n    \"\"\"\n    flag = \"-fy\" if fix else \"-fn\"\n    return run([\"e2fsck\", flag, str(fs_file)], capture=True, echo=False, check=False).returncode\n\n\ndef ext_min_resize(fs_file: Path) -> int:\n    \"\"\"Shrink ext `fs_file` to its minimum with `resize2fs -M`; return new bytes.\n\n    - resize2fs requires a clean fs, so `e2fsck` runs first (exit 0 =\n      clean, 1 = fixed, both fine; >=2 is a real error),\n    - the new size is read back from dumpe2fs after the resize.\n    \"\"\"\n    rc = e2fsck(fs_file, fix=True)\n    if rc >= 2:\n        raise SystemExit(f\"imglib: e2fsck failed on {fs_file} (exit {rc})\")\n    run([\"resize2fs\", \"-M\", str(fs_file)], capture=True)\n    return ext_size(fs_file)\n\n\ndef ext_grow(fs_file: Path) -> int:\n    \"\"\"Grow ext `fs_file` to fill its container with `resize2fs`; return new bytes.\n\n    The inverse of `ext_min_resize`: `resize2fs` with no size argument\n    expands the filesystem to fill the file (or partition). Used by the\n    write app's expand — after the partition is grown, the rootfs is\n    grown to fill it.\n\n    - resize2fs requires a clean fs, so `e2fsck` runs first (exit 0 =\n      clean, 1 = fixed, both fine; >=2 is a real error).\n    \"\"\"\n    rc = e2fsck(fs_file, fix=True)\n    if rc >= 2:\n        raise SystemExit(f\"imglib: e2fsck failed on {fs_file} (exit {rc})\")\n    run([\"resize2fs\", str(fs_file)], capture=True)\n    return ext_size(fs_file)\n\n\ndef fat_at(img: Path, off: int) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Return the mtools `image@@offset` selector for the FAT at `off`.\"\"\"\n    return f\"{img}@@{off}\"\n\n\ndef fat_list(node) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Root-directory entry names of a FAT filesystem via mtools (rootless).\n\n    - `node` is an mtools selector — a device path (`/dev/sdb1`) or an\n      `image@@offset` from fat_at,\n    - the FAT counterpart of fs_list (ext via debugfs); `mdir -b` prints\n      full paths, of which we return the basenames.\n    \"\"\"\n    cp = run([\"mdir\", \"-i\", str(node), \"-b\", \"::/\"], capture=True, echo=False, check=False)\n    return [os.path.basename(line.rstrip(\"/\"))\n            for line in cp.stdout.splitlines() if line.strip()]\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/nps-gnc/bdimager/src/bdimager/imgcfg.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Shared config plumbing for the block-device image-builder apps.\n\nThe builder is split into standalone apps — `build` (rootless),\n`capture`, and `write` — that each own their CLI (argparse) but share how\nsettings are sourced. This module is that shared layer:\n\n- load the app's `bd-config.toml` tables (e.g. `[image]` + `[build]`)\n  over a built-in default map, erroring on an unknown key,\n- merge file < `BD_<KEY>` env < CLI flag into a `{key: (value, source)}`\n  plan so `--dry-run` can show where each value won,\n- generate the per-key CLI flags and print the resolved settings block.\n\nSettings map 1:1 to CLI flags — identical names (`compress` <->\n`--compress`). Precedence, lowest to highest: the config tables, then an\n`BD_<KEY>` env var, then the matching CLI flag. The config file defaults\nto `bd-config.toml` in the current working directory; each app's\n`--config` overrides it.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport argparse\nimport os\nimport tomllib\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\n# Default config: bd-config.toml in the current working directory. An\n# installed `bd-*` tool reads the config of the project it is run in;\n# `--config` overrides it. A missing file falls back to built-in defaults.\nDEFAULT_CONFIG = Path(\"bd-config.toml\")\n\n# Canonical schema: every valid key in each bd-config.toml table. Used to\n# reject typos (an unknown key in a table) while still letting an app read\n# only the subset of a shared table it needs — e.g. capture reads [image]\n# but not its `output`, yet `output` must not look like a typo there.\nTABLE_KEYS = {\n    \"image\": {\"images_dir\", \"mother\", \"output\"},\n    \"build\": {\"shrink\", \"compress\"},\n    \"capture\": {\"source\"},\n    \"write\": {\"target\", \"target_byid\", \"expand\", \"allow_yes\"},\n}\n\n# BD_* env values that count as true / false (case-insensitive). Anything\n# else is a hard error so a typo never silently flips a toggle.\n_TRUE = {\"1\", \"true\", \"yes\", \"on\"}\n_FALSE = {\"0\", \"false\", \"no\", \"off\"}\n\n\ndef parse_bool(raw: str, source: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Coerce a BD_* env string to bool, erroring on anything unknown.\n\n    - `source` names the variable for the error message,\n    - accepts the _TRUE / _FALSE token sets, case-insensitively.\n    \"\"\"\n    token = raw.strip().lower()\n    if token in _TRUE:\n        return True\n    if token in _FALSE:\n        return False\n    raise SystemExit(\n        f\"imgcfg: {source}={raw!r} is not a boolean \"\n        f\"(use one of {sorted(_TRUE)} / {sorted(_FALSE)})\"\n    )\n\n\ndef load_config(path: Path, sections: tuple, defaults: dict) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Return `defaults` overlaid by the named `sections` of the config.\n\n    - `sections` are the `bd-config.toml` tables the app reads (e.g.\n      `(\"image\", \"build\")`); later sections win on a key overlap,\n    - a missing file is fine — fall back to `defaults` entirely so\n      `--dry-run` works on a bare checkout,\n    - a key not in a read section's canonical schema (`TABLE_KEYS`) is an\n      error (a typo); a valid key the app simply doesn't resolve — e.g.\n      capture reading [image] but not its `output` — is kept out of the\n      plan, not flagged.\n    \"\"\"\n    merged = dict(defaults)\n    if not path.exists():\n        return merged\n    doc = tomllib.loads(path.read_text())\n    for section in sections:\n        table = doc.get(section, {})\n        unknown = set(table) - TABLE_KEYS.get(section, set())\n        if unknown:\n            raise SystemExit(\n                f\"imgcfg: {path} [{section}] has unknown key(s): \"\n                f\"{', '.join(sorted(unknown))}\"\n            )\n        merged.update({k: v for k, v in table.items() if k in defaults})\n    return merged\n\n\ndef resolve(args: argparse.Namespace, file_cfg: dict,\n            str_keys: tuple, bool_keys: tuple) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Merge file < BD_* env < CLI into a {key: (value, source)} plan.\n\n    - `source` records where the winning value came from (\"config\",\n      \"env\", or \"flag\") so `--dry-run` can show the override chain,\n    - CLI flags default to None (unset) so an absent flag falls through\n      to env, then to the file default.\n    \"\"\"\n    resolved: dict = {}\n    for key in (*str_keys, *bool_keys):\n        value = file_cfg[key]\n        source = \"config\"\n\n        env_name = f\"BD_{key.upper()}\"\n        if env_name in os.environ:\n            raw = os.environ[env_name]\n            value = parse_bool(raw, env_name) if key in bool_keys else raw\n            source = \"env\"\n\n        cli = getattr(args, key)\n        if cli is not None:\n            value = cli\n            source = \"flag\"\n\n        resolved[key] = (value, source)\n    return resolved\n\n\ndef add_arguments(parser: argparse.ArgumentParser,\n                  str_keys: tuple, bool_keys: tuple) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Add one config-override flag per key to `parser`.\n\n    - string keys take a value (`--key`); bool keys use\n      BooleanOptionalAction so each gets a `--key` / `--no-key` pair,\n    - every flag defaults to None (unset) so `resolve` can tell an absent\n      flag from an explicit one and apply the precedence chain.\n    \"\"\"\n    for key in str_keys:\n        parser.add_argument(\n            f\"--{key.replace('_', '-')}\", default=None,\n            help=f\"override config {key}\",\n        )\n    for key in bool_keys:\n        parser.add_argument(\n            f\"--{key.replace('_', '-')}\", default=None,\n            action=argparse.BooleanOptionalAction,\n            help=f\"override config {key}\",\n        )\n\n\ndef img_path(plan: dict, key: str) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Resolve a `mother` / `output` plan value against `images_dir`.\n\n    A bare filename (no directory part) sits under `images_dir`; a value\n    that already carries a path — relative or absolute — is used as-is.\n    Shared by the apps that move these artifacts (build, capture, write).\n    \"\"\"\n    p = Path(plan[key][0])\n    if p.parent == Path(\".\"):\n        return Path(plan[\"images_dir\"][0]) / p\n    return p\n\n\ndef print_settings(config_path: Path, plan: dict,\n                   str_keys: tuple, bool_keys: tuple, prog: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Print the resolved settings block: each value and where it won.\n\n    - the header notes whether the config file was present,\n    - string keys show `repr`, bool keys show `on` / `off`; the `[source]`\n      column is `config` / `env` / `flag`.\n    \"\"\"\n    exists = \"present\" if config_path.exists() else \"missing, using built-in defaults\"\n    print(f\"{prog} plan (config: {config_path} — {exists})\")\n    print(\"\")\n    print(\"settings:\")\n    for key in (*str_keys, *bool_keys):\n        value, source = plan[key]\n        shown = repr(value) if key in str_keys else (\"on\" if value else \"off\")\n        print(f\"  {key:<22} {shown:<26} [{source}]\")\n"
}

dd.\n\n    - real mode: `sudo dd if=<src_img> of=<dest_dev>` — root, since\n      writing a block device needs it; `conv=fsync` flushes before return,\n    - `test_mode`: write the image bytes into = \"r+b\" if Path(dest_dev).exists() else \"wb\"\n        with open(dest_dev, mode) as f:\n            f.write(data)\n        print(f\"  write: wrote {src_img} -> {dest_dev} (test-mode)\")\n partition and its ext rootfs are grown to reclaim the free\n    space:\n\n    - real mode: `sudo sfdisk` extends the last partition, then `sudo\n      resize2fs` grows the live filesystem to fill str) -> list[dict]:\n    \"\"\"Return the partition child nodes (kname + fstype) of `device`.\"\"\"\n    kname = os.path.basename(os.path.realpath(device))\n    for d in _lsblk(\"KNAME,FSTYPE,TYPE\"):\n        if d[\"kname\"] == kname:\n            return [c for c in d.get(\"children\", []) if c.get(\"type\") == \"part\"]\n    return []\n\n\ndef preview_partitions(device: str) -> list[dict]:\n    \"\"\"List each partition of `device` with its fs type and root entries.\n\n    Rootless content peek (debugfs for ext, mtools for FAT) so the human\n    sees what's on the device before a destructive write; each entry\n    flags top-level system markers (etc/, boot/, home/, ...).\n    \"\"\"\n    out = []\n    for p in _device_partitions(device):\n        node = f\"/dev/{p['kname']}\"\n        fstype = (p.get(\"fstype\") or \"\").lower()\n        if fstype.startswith(\"ext\"):\n            names = [e[\"name\"] for e in imglib.fs_list(Path(node), \"/\")]\n        elif fstype in (\"vfat\", \"fat\", \"msdos\"):\n            names = imglib.fat_list(node)\n        else:\n            names = []\n        system = sorted({n.lower() for n in names} & _SYSTEM_MARKERS)\n        out.append({\"node\": node, \"fstype\": fstype or \"?\",\n                    \"entries\": names, \"system\": system})\n    return out\n\n\ndef confirm(device: str, action: str, *, allow_yes: bool, yes_flag: bool) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Show the target summary and require confirmation for a device write.\n\n    - prints the device and each partition's root preview, flagging any\n      that look like a system / data filesystem,\n    - the prompt is skipped only when BOTH `allow_yes` (bd-config.toml,\n      the persistent permission) and `yes_flag` (the per-run `--yes`) are\n      set — neither alone suffices,\n    - `--yes` without `allow_yes` is a hard error (checked first), so a\n      misconfigured skip fails loud instead of silently dropping to a\n      prompt that might be answered on autopilot.\n    \"\"\"\n    if yes_flag and not allow_yes:\n        raise SystemExit(\n            \"devsafe: --yes requires [write].allow_yes = true in bd-config.toml\")\n    print(f\"\\n=== {action}: {device} ===\")\n    for p in preview_partitions(device):\n        warn = f\"   [SYSTEM: {', '.join(p['system'])}]\" if p[\"system\"] else \"\"\n        listing = \", \".join(p[\"entries\"]) or \"(empty)\"\n        print(f\"  {p['node']} ({p['fstype']}): {listing}{warn}\")\n    if allow_yes and yes_flag:\n        print(\"devsafe: allow_yes + --yes set — skipping prompt.\")\n        return\n    reply = input(f\"\\nProceed to {action} {device}? type 'yes' to confirm: \").strip()\n    if reply != \"yes\":\n picking a capture/write target: shows each disk's\n    by-id alias (the handle to pass as the second target) and flags any\n    that back a mount (the system disk, which would be refused).\n != \"disk\":\n            continue\n        kname = d[\"kname\"]\n        flag = \"   [SYSTEM/MOUNTED — would be refused]\" if kname in protected else \"\"\n        print(f\"  /dev/{kname}                print(f\"      by-id: /dev/disk/by-id/{alias}\")\n        else:\n            print(\"      by-id: (none — udev/mdev may not cover this device)\")\n    return 0\n"

  "content": "\"\"\"Build / verify a small synthetic Pi-like `.img` for testing the\nbuilder's offline inject + finalize — rootless, stdlib + imglib only.\n\nA real mother image is multi-GB and only exists after a card capture.\nTo exercise the inject + finalize passes on their own, this helper makes\na ~160 MB image with the same shape as a Pi card — a FAT boot partition\nplus an ext4 root — seeded with a few generic files: a survivor that\nmust persist across build, and a stale file an inject can overwrite.\n`verify` reports the finalize outcome (e2fsck clean, shrunk, xz valid);\ninject-specific assertions live in the test that knows what it injected.\n\nLike the build itself, it needs no root: `sfdisk` writes the table,\n`mke2fs` + `debugfs` build and seed a standalone ext4 root that is then\nspliced into the image, and `mtools` formats + seeds the FAT boot via\nits `image@@offset` syntax.\n\n    python3 -m bdimager.mk_test_image build images/mother.img\n    bd-build --mother images/mother.img --output images/deploy.img \\\\\n        --shrink --compress\n    python3 -m bdimager.mk_test_image verify images/deploy.img\n\"\"\"\n\nimport argparse\nimport subprocess\nimport tempfile\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom . import imglib\n\nTOTAL_BYTES = 160 * 1024 * 1024  # 160 MB: FAT32 boot (~47 MB) + ext4 root\n\n# DOS table: ~47 MB FAT boot (type c) then the rest ext4 (type 83),\n# matching a Pi card's p1/p2 layout.\nSFDISK_SCRIPT = \"\"\"label: dos\nunit: sectors\nstart=2048, size=96256, type=c\ntype=83\n\"\"\"\n\n# Generic files seeded on the ext4 root. Paths are absolute within the\n# filesystem.\n#\n# - the survivor must persist unchanged across a build (a sanity anchor\n#   that build never clobbers untouched files),\n# - the stale file gives an inject test an overwrite target — its content\n#   must change when `--add <host>:/opt/app/config.txt` runs.\nSURVIVOR_PATH = \"/etc/hostname\"\nSURVIVOR_CONTENT = \"bdimager-test\\n\"\nSTALE_PATH = \"/opt/app/config.txt\"\nSTALE_CONTENT = \"OLD STALE CONFIG\\n\"\n\nSEED_ROOT = {\n    SURVIVOR_PATH: SURVIVOR_CONTENT,\n    STALE_PATH: STALE_CONTENT,\n    \"/etc/os-release\": 'NAME=\"bdimager-test\"\\nID=test\\n',\n    # Some bulk content so the pre-shrink rootfs is non-trivial and the\n    # finalize shrink visibly reclaims space.\n    \"/var/lib/data/blob.txt\": \"filler line for the test rootfs\\n\" * 200,\n}\n\n# Token files written to the FAT boot partition.\nSEED_BOOT = {\n    \"config.txt\": \"dtoverlay=uart0-pi5\\n\",\n    \"cmdline.txt\": \"console=serial0,115200 root=/dev/mmcblk0p2\\n\",\n}\n\n\ndef pick_boot_root(img: Path) -> tuple[dict, dict]:\n    \"\"\"Return (boot, root) partition dicts by MBR type (FAT 'c', ext '83').\n\n    Used at build time, before any filesystem exists to probe — so it\n    keys off the partition table type, not a filesystem magic.\n    \"\"\"\n    fat = {\"1\", \"4\", \"6\", \"b\", \"c\", \"e\", \"ef\"}\n    parts = imglib.partitions(img)\n    boot = next(p for p in parts if p[\"type\"].lower().lstrip(\"0\") in fat)\n    root = next(p for p in parts if p[\"type\"] == \"83\")\n    return boot, root\n\n\ndef seed_root(root_fs: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"mke2fs has run on `root_fs`; seed it with the SEED_ROOT files.\n\n    Stages each file's content to a host temp file, then drives one\n    `debugfs -w` batch of mkdir + write commands. debugfs mkdir is not\n    recursive, so every ancestor directory is created, shallowest first.\n    \"\"\"\n    dirs: set[str] = set()\n    for path in SEED_ROOT:\n        parent = Path(path).parent\n        while str(parent) != \"/\":\n            dirs.add(str(parent))\n            parent = parent.parent\n    ordered = sorted(dirs, key=lambda d: d.count(\"/\"))\n\n    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix=\"mk-seed-\") as tmp:\n        cmds = [f\"mkdir {d}\" for d in ordered]\n        for i, (path, content) in enumerate(SEED_ROOT.items()):\n            host = Path(tmp) / f\"seed{i}\"\n            host.write_text(content)\n            cmds.append(f\"write {host} {path}\")\n        imglib.debugfs_batch(root_fs, cmds)\n\n\ndef build(img: Path) -> int:\n    \"\"\"Create a seeded synthetic Pi-like image at `img` (overwrites).\"\"\"\n    img.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    with open(img, \"wb\") as f:\n        f.truncate(TOTAL_BYTES)\n    imglib.run([\"sfdisk\", str(img)], stdin=SFDISK_SCRIPT, capture=True)\n\n    boot, root = pick_boot_root(img)\n\n    # Build + seed a standalone ext4 root, then splice it into the image.\n    root_fs = Path(tempfile.mkstemp(prefix=\"mk-root-\", suffix=\".ext4\")[1])\n    try:\n        with open(root_fs, \"wb\") as f:\n            f.truncate(root[\"length\"])\n        imglib.run([\"mke2fs\", \"-F\", \"-q\", \"-t\", \"ext4\", str(root_fs)], capture=True)\n        seed_root(root_fs)\n        imglib.splice_region(img, root[\"off\"], root_fs)\n    finally:\n        root_fs.unlink(missing_ok=True)\n\n    # Format + seed the FAT boot partition in place via mtools @@offset.\n    selector = imglib.fat_at(img, boot[\"off\"])\n    imglib.run([\"mformat\", \"-i\", selector, \"-F\", \"::\"], capture=True)\n    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix=\"mk-boot-\") as tmp:\n        for name, content in SEED_BOOT.items():\n            host = Path(tmp) / name\n            host.write_text(content)\n            imglib.run(\n                [\"mcopy\", \"-i\", selector, \"-o\", str(host), f\"::{name}\"], capture=True\n            )\n\n    print(\n        f\"mk_test_image: built seeded image {img} ({TOTAL_BYTES // (1024 * 1024)} MB)\"\n    )\n    return 0\n\n\ndef _entry(root_fs: Path, path: str) -> dict | None:\n    \"\"\"Return the directory entry for `path`, or None if it's absent.\"\"\"\n    parent, _, name = path.rpartition(\"/\")\n    for e in imglib.fs_list(root_fs, parent or \"/\"):\n        if e[\"name\"] == name:\n            return e\n    return None\n\n\ndef verify(img: Path) -> int:\n    \"\"\"Extract `img`'s root and report finalize outcome; 0 if all clean.\n\n    Checks the generic build outcome — survivor preserved, rootfs clean,\n    shrunk smaller than the synthetic mother, and an xz that passes its\n    integrity check. Inject-specific assertions are the caller's job\n    (it knows what it added), via imglib.fs_read / fs_exists.\n    \"\"\"\n    _boot, root, _parts = imglib.find_boot_root(img)\n    if root is None:\n        raise SystemExit(f\"mk_test_image: no ext root partition in {img}\")\n\n    root_fs = imglib.extract_region(img, root[\"off\"], root[\"length\"])\n    failures = []\n    try:\n        rc = imglib.e2fsck(root_fs, fix=False)\n        print(f\"e2fsck: exit {rc} ({'clean' if rc == 0 else 'errors'})\")\n        if rc != 0:\n            failures.append(\"e2fsck\")\n\n        print(\"\\nverify build:\")\n        # Survivor preserved unchanged.\n        survivor = imglib.fs_read(root_fs, SURVIVOR_PATH)\n        ok = survivor == SURVIVOR_CONTENT\n        print(f\"  {'PASS' if ok else 'FAIL'} survivor {SURVIVOR_PATH}\")\n        if not ok:\n            failures.append(SURVIVOR_PATH)\n\n        print(\"\\nverify finalization:\")\n        # shrink: the deploy image is smaller than the synthetic mother.\n        img_size = img.stat().st_size\n        ok = img_size < TOTAL_BYTES\n        print(\n            f\"  {'PASS' if ok else 'FAIL'} shrink image {img_size} B < mother {TOTAL_BYTES} B\"\n        )\n        if not ok:\n            failures.append(\"shrink\")\n        # xz: the compressed image exists and passes its integrity check.\n        xz = Path(f\"{img}.xz\")\n        ok = (\n            xz.exists()\n            and subprocess.run([\"xz\", \"-t\", str(xz)], capture_output=True).returncode\n            == 0\n        )\n        print(\n            f\"  {'PASS' if ok else 'FAIL'} xz     {xz} ({'ok' if xz.exists() else 'missing'})\"\n        )\n        if not ok:\n            failures.append(str(xz))\n    finally:\n        root_fs.unlink(missing_ok=True)\n\n    if failures:\n        print(f\"\\nmk_test_image: {len(failures)} check(s) FAILED\")\n        return 1\n    print(\"\\nmk_test_image: all checks passed\")\n    return 0\n\n\ndef verify_expanded(img: Path) -> int:\n    \"\"\"Check an expanded card: root partition + rootfs grew to fill it.\n\n    Asserts the write app's expand worked — the last partition reaches\n    (within a sector of) the end of the card, the ext rootfs fills that\n    partition, e2fsck is clean, and the survivor file lived through the\n    grow.\n    \"\"\"\n    _boot, root, _parts = imglib.find_boot_root(img)\n    if root is None:\n        raise SystemExit(f\"mk_test_image: no ext root partition in {img}\")\n    img_size = img.stat().st_size\n    sector = imglib.sector_size(img)\n    failures = []\n\n    print(\"verify expand:\")\n    root_end = root[\"off\"] + root[\"length\"]\n    ok = root_end >= img_size - sector\n    print(f\"  {'PASS' if ok else 'FAIL'} partition fills card \"\n          f\"(root end {root_end} B vs card {img_size} B)\")\n    if not ok:\n        failures.append(\"partition-fill\")\n\n    root_fs = imglib.extract_region(img, root[\"off\"], root[\"length\"])\n    try:\n        rc = imglib.e2fsck(root_fs, fix=False)\n        ok = rc == 0\n        print(f\"  {'PASS' if ok else 'FAIL'} e2fsck clean (exit {rc})\")\n        if not ok:\n            failures.append(\"e2fsck\")\n        fs_size = imglib.ext_size(root_fs)\n        ok = fs_size >= root[\"length\"] - 1024 * 1024\n        print(f\"  {'PASS' if ok else 'FAIL'} rootfs fills partition \"\n              f\"(fs {fs_size} B vs part {root['length']} B)\")\n        if not ok:\n            failures.append(\"fs-fill\")\n        ok = imglib.fs_read(root_fs, SURVIVOR_PATH) == SURVIVOR_CONTENT\n        print(f\"  {'PASS' if ok else 'FAIL'} survivor survived ({SURVIVOR_PATH})\")\n        if not ok:\n            failures.append(\"survivor\")\n    finally:\n        root_fs.unlink(missing_ok=True)\n\n    if failures:\n        print(f\"\\nmk_test_image: expand verify — {len(failures)} check(s) FAILED\")\n        return 1\n    print(\"\\nmk_test_image: expand verify all passed\")\n    return 0\n\n\ndef main() -> int:\n    \"\"\"Parse the build/verify/verify-expanded subcommand and dispatch (rootless).\"\"\"\n    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(\n        prog=\"mk_test_image\", description=__doc__.splitlines()[0]\n    )\n    sub = p.add_subparsers(dest=\"cmd\", required=True)\n    for name in (\"build\", \"verify\", \"verify-expanded\"):\n        sub.add_parser(name).add_argument(\"img\", type=Path)\n    args = p.parse_args()\n    if args.cmd == \"build\":\n        return build(args.img)\n    if args.cmd == \"verify\":\n        return verify(args.img)\n    return verify_expanded(args.img)\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    raise SystemExit(main())\n"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/nps-gnc/bdimager/bd-config.toml",
  "old_string": "[project]\nversion = \"0.3.0+0\"",
  "new_string": "[project]\nversion = \"0.3.0+0\"\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Block-device image builder — three apps share this config:\n#   bd-build   — rootless inject + finalize (shrink / compress)\n#   bd-capture — read a source device into the mother image\n#   bd-write   — write the deployable image to a target device\n#\n# Every key is the default for the matching CLI flag — identical names\n# (TOML `snake_case` <-> CLI `--snake-case`). Precedence, lowest to\n# highest: this file < BD_* env var < CLI flag. The file's default\n# location is the current working directory; `--config` overrides it.\n#\n# Tables: [image] holds the handoff artifacts every app shares; [build] /\n# [capture] / [write] hold each app's own knobs. Each app reads [image]\n# plus its own table and errors on an unknown key in either.\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n# Shared handoff artifacts: capture writes `mother`, build reads it and\n# writes `output`, write reads that.\n[image]\n\n# Local directory holding the `.img` files. A bare-filename `mother` /\n# `output` sits under it; a value with a path is used as-is.\nimages_dir = \"images\"\n\n# Mother image: the source `.img` build transforms into a deployable\n# image. capture writes the captured device here.\nmother = \"mother.img\"\n\n# Output: the deployable `.img` build writes. Left as the bare image\n# unless `compress` xz-packs it to `<output>.xz`.\noutput = \"deploy.img\"\n\n\n# build app: the rootless inject + finalize pipeline. File/dir injection\n# is supplied per-run via `--add <src>:<dest>` (or `@file`), not here.\n[build]\n\n# Finalize: shrink the root partition to its used size, then xz-compress\n# the output image.\nshrink = true\ncompress = true\n\n\n# capture app: dd a physical device into `mother`.\n[capture]\n\n# Source block device to capture — an SD card, USB stick, NVMe, or disk\n# — named as `/dev/xx` or a `/dev/disk/by-id/yy` handle. Empty = no\n# capture (build uses the existing mother). Capture is a read, so no\n# two-key / confirm — that safety guards the destructive write, not this.\nsource = \"\"\n\n\n# write app: dd `output` to a target device. Destructive — writes a\n# physical device, behind the full devsafe gate.\n[write]\n\n# Target device to write, as `/dev/xx`. Empty = no write. Destructive,\n# so write requires two-key targeting (this plus `target_byid`) + a confirm.\ntarget = \"\"\n\n# Second targeting key: the `/dev/disk/by-id/yy` handle for the same\n# device. write aborts unless `target` and `target_byid` resolve to the\n# same major:minor, so a stale `/dev/sdX` can't slip a wrong-device write.\ntarget_byid = \"\"\n\n# After writing, grow the root partition + filesystem to fill the target\n# device (the shrunk image leaves the rest of the card unused). Off with\n# `--no-expand` to leave the image at its written size.\nexpand = true\n\n# Permit the --yes flag to skip the interactive confirm before a\n# destructive write. Two-factor by design: skipping needs BOTH this (the\n# persistent permission) AND --yes on the command line (the per-run\n# intent); --yes without this is a hard error. There is deliberately no\n# --allow-yes flag — the permission can only be granted here.\nallow_yes = false"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/nps-gnc/bdimager/tests/test_core.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Tests for the rootless storage + config core (0.3.0-1).\n\n- imgcfg: config load / schema rejection / precedence — pure, no tools,\n- imglib + mk_test_image: a rootless round-trip over a synthetic image\n  (sfdisk / mke2fs / debugfs / mtools) proving the in-file edit path.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport argparse\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom bdimager import imgcfg, imglib, mk_test_image\n\n# --- imgcfg ---------------------------------------------------------------\n\n_DEFAULTS = {\"images_dir\": \"images\", \"mother\": \"mother.img\", \"shrink\": True}\n\n\ndef test_load_config_missing_returns_defaults(tmp_path):\n    \"\"\"A missing config file falls back to the built-in defaults entirely.\"\"\"\n    cfg = imgcfg.load_config(tmp_path / \"absent.toml\", (\"image\", \"build\"), _DEFAULTS)\n    assert cfg == _DEFAULTS\n\n\ndef test_load_config_overlays_known_keys(tmp_path):\n    \"\"\"Known keys in a read section overlay the defaults.\"\"\"\n    p = tmp_path / \"bd-config.toml\"\n    p.write_text('[image]\\nimages_dir = \"imgs\"\\n[build]\\nshrink = false\\n')\n    cfg = imgcfg.load_config(p, (\"image\", \"build\"), _DEFAULTS)\n    assert cfg[\"images_dir\"] == \"imgs\"\n    assert cfg[\"shrink\"] is False\n    assert cfg[\"mother\"] == \"mother.img\"  # untouched default\n\n\ndef test_load_config_rejects_unknown_key(tmp_path):\n    \"\"\"An unknown key in a read section is a hard error (a typo).\"\"\"\n    p = tmp_path / \"bd-config.toml\"\n    p.write_text('[build]\\nshrnk = true\\n')  # typo\n    with pytest.raises(SystemExit):\n        imgcfg.load_config(p, (\"build\",), _DEFAULTS)\n\n\ndef test_resolve_precedence(monkeypatch):\n    \"\"\"Precedence is file < BD_* env < CLI flag, recorded in the source.\"\"\"\n    file_cfg = {\"images_dir\": \"images\", \"shrink\": True}\n    # env overrides file\n    monkeypatch.setenv(\"BD_IMAGES_DIR\", \"from-env\")\n    # flag overrides env; absent flag (None) falls through\n    args = argparse.Namespace(images_dir=\"from-flag\", shrink=None)\n    plan = imgcfg.resolve(args, file_cfg, (\"images_dir\",), (\"shrink\",))\n    assert plan[\"images_dir\"] == (\"from-flag\", \"flag\")\n    assert plan[\"shrink\"] == (True, \"config\")\n\n\ndef test_resolve_env_only(monkeypatch):\n    \"\"\"With no flag, a BD_* env var wins over the file value.\"\"\"\n    file_cfg = {\"shrink\": True}\n    monkeypatch.setenv(\"BD_SHRINK\", \"off\")\n    args = argparse.Namespace(shrink=None)\n    plan = imgcfg.resolve(args, file_cfg, (), (\"shrink\",))\n    assert plan[\"shrink\"] == (False, \"env\")\n\n\ndef test_parse_bool_rejects_garbage():\n    \"\"\"A non-boolean BD_* value fails loud rather than silently flipping.\"\"\"\n    assert imgcfg.parse_bool(\"yes\", \"BD_SHRINK\") is True\n    with pytest.raises(SystemExit):\n        imgcfg.parse_bool(\"maybe\", \"BD_SHRINK\")\n\n\n# --- imglib round-trip ----------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_synthetic_image_roundtrip(tmp_path):\n    \"\"\"Build a synthetic image, then edit its ext root + read its FAT boot.\n\n    Exercises the whole rootless toolkit: sfdisk table, mke2fs root,\n    debugfs read/write, splice extract/back, mtools FAT listing.\n    \"\"\"\n    img = tmp_path / \"mother.img\"\n    assert mk_test_image.build(img) == 0\n\n    boot, root, parts = imglib.find_boot_root(img)\n    assert boot is not None and root is not None\n    assert len(parts) == 2\n\n    # ext root: clean fsck + seeded survivor reads back.\n    root_fs = imglib.extract_region(img, root[\"off\"], root[\"length\"])\n    try:\n        assert imglib.e2fsck(root_fs, fix=False) == 0\n        assert imglib.fs_read(root_fs, mk_test_image.SURVIVOR_PATH) == \\\n            mk_test_image.SURVIVOR_CONTENT\n        assert imglib.fs_exists(root_fs, mk_test_image.STALE_PATH)\n\n        # Inject a new file, splice back, and confirm it survives a re-read.\n        marker = tmp_path / \"marker.txt\"\n        marker.write_text(\"injected\\n\")\n        imglib.debugfs_batch(root_fs, [f\"write {marker} /marker.txt\"])\n        imglib.splice_region(img, root[\"off\"], root_fs)\n    finally:\n        root_fs.unlink(missing_ok=True)\n\n    reread = imglib.extract_region(img, root[\"off\"], root[\"length\"])\n    try:\n        assert imglib.fs_read(reread, \"/marker.txt\") == \"injected\\n\"\n    finally:\n        reread.unlink(missing_ok=True)\n\n    # FAT boot: the seeded boot tokens list back via mtools.\n    names = imglib.fat_list(imglib.fat_at(img, boot[\"off\"]))\n    assert \"config.txt\" in names\n    assert \"cmdline.txt\" in names\n"
}
